Perfect! I've now read all the files. Let me provide you with a comprehensive analysis of the relationships and overlaps between CLAUDE.md and CONTEXT.md files.

---

## Complete File Contents & Cross-Reference Analysis

### FILES FOUND (8 total):
1. **Root CLAUDE.md** - `/Users/sumeet/matrix/web/argus/CLAUDE.md` (empty/minimal)
2. **argus/CLAUDE.md** - `/Users/sumeet/matrix/web/argus/argus/CLAUDE.md` (main worker spec)
3. **argus/CONTEXT.md** - `/Users/sumeet/matrix/web/argus/argus/CONTEXT.md` (routing table)
4. **domains/system/CONTEXT.md** - System domain contract
5. **domains/validation/CONTEXT.md** - Validation domain contract
6. **domains/knowledge/CONTEXT.md** - Knowledge domain contract
7. **domains/tasks/CONTEXT.md** - Tasks domain contract
8. **domains/chat/CONTEXT.md** - Chat domain contract

---

## OVERLAP ANALYSIS

### 1. **Routing Table (Primary Overlap)**

**argus/CLAUDE.md** (Lines 7-16) contains a "Folder Map" that lists all domains and their CONTEXT.md locations:
```
- domains/tasks/CONTEXT.md — task management rules, priorities, project routing
- domains/system/CONTEXT.md — architecture, VPS services
- domains/validation/CONTEXT.md — health checks, audits
- domains/knowledge/CONTEXT.md — daily roundup, vault entity management, cross-linking
- domains/chat/CONTEXT.md — communication style (read for narration guidance)
```

**argus/CONTEXT.md** (Lines 1-16) is a **Routing** table with an identical structure:
```
| Domain | Path | When to load |
| chat | domains/chat/CONTEXT.md | Always — read this on every message |
| tasks | domains/tasks/CONTEXT.md | Creating, listing, updating, or discussing tasks |
| system | domains/system/CONTEXT.md | Questions about architecture, VPS, services, or how you work |
| validation | domains/validation/CONTEXT.md | Health checks, system validation, debugging |
| knowledge | domains/knowledge/CONTEXT.md | Daily roundup, vault entity management, cross-linking |
```

**OVERLAP**: Both files describe the same 5 domains with identical paths. The main difference is:
- CLAUDE.md is **narrative style** (bullet points with explanations)
- CONTEXT.md is **structured table** with "when to load" conditions

---

### 2. **Domain Descriptions (Direct Correlations)**

Each domain has a CONTEXT.md file that describes when it's active and what it does. All of these are **referenced** by both CLAUDE.md and the routing CONTEXT.md:

| Domain | CONTEXT.md Location | "When active" rule |
|--------|---------------------|-------------------|
| **chat** | domains/chat/CONTEXT.md | Always — you communicate results back to the user via the chat model |
| **tasks** | domains/tasks/CONTEXT.md | Creating, listing, updating, moving, or discussing tasks and projects |
| **system** | domains/system/CONTEXT.md | Questions about Argus architecture, VPS services, infrastructure |
| **validation** | domains/validation/CONTEXT.md | Health checks, system audits, debugging, investigations |
| **knowledge** | domains/knowledge/CONTEXT.md | Daily roundup, vault entity management, cross-linking |

---

### 3. **Reference Material (Secondary Structure)**

**argus/CONTEXT.md** mentions reference files that are NOT in CLAUDE.md:
```
| references/architecture.md | Full system architecture, VPS services, stack, design philosophy |
| references/telegram.md | Telegram bot commands, constraints, how to add new commands |
```

**domains/system/CONTEXT.md** specifically calls out:
```
Inputs: references/architecture.md — full system architecture, services, stack, design philosophy
```

**OVERLAP**: CONTEXT.md serves as the navigation hub that directs to both domain-specific CONTEXT.md files AND reference files, while CLAUDE.md only mentions domain CONTEXT.md files.

---

### 4. **Special Handling: Artifacts (Validation Domain)**

**domains/validation/CONTEXT.md** (unique layer):
- Defines Layer 4 artifacts stored in `domains/validation/artifacts/`
- Lists procedures: `health.md`, `audit.md`, `inv.md`
- This is the ONLY domain with persistent output artifacts

**OVERLAP**: Not mentioned in other files — validation is the only domain with explicit artifact storage.

---

### 5. **Special Handling: Vault/Wiki (Knowledge Domain)**

**domains/knowledge/CONTEXT.md** references:
```
- references/second-brain.md for full vault structure and ownership rules
- domains/knowledge/roundup.md — daily journal roundup process
```

**OVERLAP**: Knowledge domain uniquely references wiki/ files and has its own roundup procedure.

---

### 6. **Communication Contract (Chat Domain)**

**domains/chat/CONTEXT.md** specifies:
- Narration rules for the worker
- Two-tier system behavior (worker → chat model → user)
- Output format requirements
- **This is unique**: No other domain specifies communication style

---

### 7. **Task Project Routing (Tasks Domain - Unique)**

**domains/tasks/CONTEXT.md** contains rules for which project tasks go into:
- Argus project (extending Argus)
- Personal project (fitness, errands, habits)
- Research project (investigations)
- Inbox project (default)

**This is task-specific logic** not mentioned in other CONTEXT files.

---

## Cross-File References Summary

**CLAUDE.md references:**
- All 5 domain CONTEXT.md files (by name)
- Does NOT reference CONTEXT.md routing table explicitly (though it's the entry point)

**CONTEXT.md (routing) references:**
- All 5 domain CONTEXT.md files (explicit paths)
- All 2 reference files (explicit paths)
- Updates note about keeping telegram.md in sync

**Each domain CONTEXT.md references:**
- Its own input/procedure files (e.g., references/architecture.md for system)
- Procedures and artifacts specific to that domain

---

## Key Insights

1. **CLAUDE.md is the entry point** — tells the worker to read domain CONTEXT.md files as needed
2. **CONTEXT.md is the navigation hub** — structured routing table for all domains + references
3. **Domain CONTEXT.md files are contracts** — specify when active, inputs, process, outputs
4. **Content overlap is intentional**:
   - CLAUDE.md: Narrative overview for human understanding
   - CONTEXT.md: Structured routing for agent dispatch logic
5. **Each domain has specialized knowledge**:
   - Tasks: project routing rules
   - Chat: narration/communication style
   - System: references architecture.md
   - Validation: defines artifact storage
   - Knowledge: references vault/wiki structure

The relationship is **hierarchical**: CLAUDE.md → CONTEXT.md (routing) → Domain CONTEXT.md files → Reference materials

1	"""Context loading — separate prompts for chat model and worker model."""
2	
3	from datetime import datetime
4	from pathlib import Path
5	
6	from .config import ARGUS_HOME
7	
8	
9	def load_file(path: Path) -> str:
10	    try:
11	        return path.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
12	    except FileNotFoundError:
13	        return ""
14	
15	
16	def _date_context() -> str:
17	    now = datetime.now()
18	    return f"\n## Current Date\nToday is {now.strftime('%A %d %B %Y')}. The time is {now.strftime('%H:%M')}.\n"
19	
20	
21	def build_chat_prompt(role: str = "admin") -> str:
22	    """Build system prompt for the fast chat model. Role-aware."""
23	    if role == "admin":
24	        chat_md = load_file(ARGUS_HOME / "prompts" / "chat.md")
25	    else:
26	        chat_md = load_file(ARGUS_HOME / "prompts" / "chat_member.md")
27	    parts = [chat_md, _date_context()]
28	    return "\n".join(parts)
29	
30	
31	def build_worker_prompt(task: str, intent: str, role: str = "admin") -> str:
32	    """Build system prompt for the worker model. Full ICM context."""
33	    claude_md = load_file(ARGUS_HOME / "CLAUDE.md")
34	    context_md = load_file(ARGUS_HOME / "CONTEXT.md")
35	    chat_domain = load_file(ARGUS_HOME / "domains" / "chat" / "CONTEXT.md")
36	    parts = [claude_md, "\n", context_md, "\n", chat_domain, _date_context()]
37	    if role != "admin":
38	        parts.append("\n## User Role\nThis user is a member (not admin). They can ONLY manage tasks on shared projects (Project Pyari, id=5). Do NOT access vault, journal, Strava, or any personal data. Do NOT read or write files. Only use task management tools.\n")
39	    if intent == "fitness":
40	        fitness_prompt = load_file(ARGUS_HOME / "prompts" / "fitness.md")
41	        parts.append(f"\n{fitness_prompt}")
42	    parts.append(f"\n## Current Task\n{task}\nIntent: {intent}")
43	    return "\n".join(parts)
44	
```

1	"""Audit — gathers all system prompts and architecture files for the worker to review."""
2	
3	import os
4	from pathlib import Path
5	
6	from .config import ARGUS_HOME
7	
8	
9	def run_audit() -> str:
10	    """Collect all prompts, domain contracts, references, and config for review."""
11	    parts = [f"# Argus System Audit — Full Architecture Dump\n"]
12	
13	    # CLAUDE.md (worker Layer 0)
14	    parts.append(_read_section("CLAUDE.md (Worker Layer 0)", ARGUS_HOME / "CLAUDE.md"))
15	
16	    # CONTEXT.md (routing table Layer 1)
17	    parts.append(_read_section("CONTEXT.md (Routing Table Layer 1)", ARGUS_HOME / "CONTEXT.md"))
18	
19	    # Chat prompt (fast model)
20	    parts.append(_read_section("prompts/chat.md (Chat Model Prompt)", ARGUS_HOME / "prompts" / "chat.md"))
21	
22	    # All domain contracts (Layer 2)
23	    domains_dir = ARGUS_HOME / "domains"
24	    if domains_dir.exists():
25	        for ctx in sorted(domains_dir.rglob("CONTEXT.md")):
26	            rel = ctx.relative_to(ARGUS_HOME)
27	            parts.append(_read_section(f"{rel} (Domain Contract)", ctx))
28	
29	    # All references (Layer 3)
30	    refs_dir = ARGUS_HOME / "references"
31	    if refs_dir.exists():
32	        for ref in sorted(refs_dir.rglob("*.md")):
33	            rel = ref.relative_to(ARGUS_HOME)
34	            parts.append(_read_section(f"{rel} (Reference)", ref))
35	
36	    # MCP config
37	    mcp_path = ARGUS_HOME / "mcp.json"
38	    if mcp_path.exists():
39	        parts.append(_read_section("mcp.json (MCP Server Config)", mcp_path))
40	
41	    # Folder structure
42	    parts.append(_folder_tree())
43	
44	    # Git status — is anything out of date?
45	    parts.append(_git_status())
46	
47	    # Instructions for the worker
48	    parts.append("""## Audit Instructions
49	Review all the content above and check for:
50	1. **Conflicting rules** — do any domain contracts contradict each other or the CLAUDE.md?
51	2. **Prompt coherence** — does the chat prompt align with how the worker behaves? Are handoff intents complete?
52	3. **Routing completeness** — does CONTEXT.md reference all domains and references that exist?
53	4. **Stale content** — are there references to tools, models, or services that no longer exist?
54	5. **Missing contracts** — are there domains or capabilities that lack a CONTEXT.md?
55	6. **Reference accuracy** — does references/architecture.md match the actual current system?
56	7. **Telegram commands** — does references/telegram.md match what's actually registered in post_init()?
57	8. **ICM compliance** — does the folder structure follow Van Clief's 5-layer model correctly?
58	9. **Staleness** — are references/architecture.md or references/telegram.md out of date with the actual code?
59	10. **Deploy sync** — is the VPS running the latest code? Check git status.
60	
61	Report findings as a structured list: what's good, what's broken, what should be fixed.""")
62	
63	    report = "\n\n".join(parts)
64	
65	    # Persist as Layer 4 artifact
66	    artifact_path = ARGUS_HOME / "domains" / "validation" / "artifacts" / "last_audit.md"
67	    artifact_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
68	    artifact_path.write_text(report, encoding="utf-8")
69	
70	    return report
71	
72	
73	def _read_section(title: str, path: Path) -> str:
74	    try:
75	        content = path.read_text(encoding="utf-8").strip()
76	        return f"## {title}\n```\n{content}\n```"
77	    except FileNotFoundError:
78	        return f"## {title}\nFILE NOT FOUND: {path}"
79	
80	
81	def _git_status() -> str:
82	    """Check git status and recent commits for staleness."""
83	    import subprocess
84	    parts = ["## Git Status"]
85	    try:
86	        status = subprocess.run(
87	            ["git", "status", "--short"], capture_output=True, text=True,
88	            timeout=5, cwd=str(ARGUS_HOME.parent)
89	        ).stdout.strip()
90	        parts.append(f"Working tree:\n```\n{status or '(clean)'}\n```")
91	
92	        log = subprocess.run(
93	            ["git", "log", "--oneline", "-10"], capture_output=True, text=True,
94	            timeout=5, cwd=str(ARGUS_HOME.parent)
95	        ).stdout.strip()
96	        parts.append(f"Recent commits:\n```\n{log}\n```")
97	
98	        # Check if HEAD matches remote
99	        local = subprocess.run(
100	            ["git", "rev-parse", "HEAD"], capture_output=True, text=True,
101	            timeout=5, cwd=str(ARGUS_HOME.parent)
102	        ).stdout.strip()
103	        remote = subprocess.run(
104	            ["git", "rev-parse", "origin/master"], capture_output=True, text=True,
105	            timeout=5, cwd=str(ARGUS_HOME.parent)
106	        ).stdout.strip()
107	        if local == remote:
108	            parts.append("Deploy status: UP TO DATE (HEAD matches origin/master)")
109	        else:
110	            parts.append(f"Deploy status: OUT OF SYNC (local={local[:8]}, remote={remote[:8]})")
111	    except Exception as e:
112	        parts.append(f"Could not check git: {e}")
113	    return "\n".join(parts)
114	
115	
116	def _folder_tree() -> str:
117	    tree = ["## Folder Structure"]
118	    for root, dirs, files in os.walk(ARGUS_HOME):
119	        # Skip data, memories, venv, __pycache__
120	        dirs[:] = [d for d in dirs if d not in ("data", "memories", "venv", "__pycache__", ".git")]
121	        level = Path(root).relative_to(ARGUS_HOME)
122	        indent = "  " * len(level.parts)
123	        tree.append(f"{indent}{Path(root).name}/")
124	        for f in sorted(files):
125	            if f.startswith(".") or f.endswith(".pyc"):
126	                continue
127	            tree.append(f"{indent}  {f}")
128	    return "\n".join(tree)
129	
```

1	# Fitness Domain — Workout Generation
2	
3	You are acting as Sumeet's personal trainer. Your job is to generate a workout plan, save it with create_workout, and return the link.
4	
5	## Step 1: Read the program and exercise library
6	
7	Do TWO things first:
8	1. `read_file("personal/health/program.md")` — current working levels, progressions, session templates
9	2. `list_exercises` — get the full exercise library with types, muscles, and equipment
10	
11	Do NOT guess his levels. Read the program. Do NOT invent exercise names — only use exercises from `list_exercises`.
12	
13	## Step 2: Check recent workout history
14	
15	Use `get_recent_workouts` to check what was trained recently:
16	- What muscle groups were trained recently
17	- How many days since the last workout
18	- Whether he needs rest or can train
19	
20	## Step 3: Decide what to recommend
21	
22	Act like a personal trainer. YOU decide what he should do today based on:
23	
24	**If he just said "/workout" or "I want to workout" without specifying:**
25	- Check what he trained last and when
26	- If he trained upper body yesterday → recommend lower body + core or an easy run
27	- If he trained full body yesterday → recommend rest or yoga/flexibility only
28	- If it's been 2+ days → recommend full body
29	- If it's been 3+ days → recommend full body, slightly higher volume
30	- If he's trained 3+ consecutive days → recommend rest day
31	- Tell him what you recommend and why before creating the workout
32	
33	**If he specified what he wants:**
34	- Follow his request but adjust based on recovery needs
35	- If he wants to do something that conflicts with recovery (e.g., push day after push day), gently suggest an alternative but respect his choice if he insists
36	
37	**If he mentioned equipment/location:**
38	- Home with bar + rings: full exercise selection
39	- Home with bar only: same minus ring exercises
40	- Park WITH pull-up bar: good — use the bar for pull-ups, dead hangs
41	- Park/away with NO bar: skip all pulling exercises entirely. Do NOT suggest inverted rows on a bench, table, or any improvised equipment — it looks ridiculous. Focus on push, legs, core, and flexibility instead.
42	- Gym: full equipment, add weighted variations
43	
44	## Step 4: Build the workout
45	
46	The workout MUST include ALL phases as separate exercises in the plan, not just in notes:
47	
48	Every exercise MUST have a `phase` field set to one of: `"warm-up"`, `"skills"`, `"main"`, or `"cool-down"`. The UI groups exercises by phase.
49	
50	Map exercise type to phase: mobility → warm-up, skill → skills, strength → main, stretch → cool-down.
51	
52	Only use exercise `name` values from `list_exercises`. Do NOT invent new exercise names.
53	
54	### Warm-up exercises (phase: "warm-up"):
55	- "Surya Namaskar" — target_sets: 3-5, phase: "warm-up", notes: "Slow pace, full breath each pose"
56	- "Wrist warm-up" — target_duration_seconds: 180, phase: "warm-up", notes: "Circles, prayer stretch, floor extensions"
57	- "Shoulder mobility" — target_duration_seconds: 180, phase: "warm-up", notes: "Band pull-aparts, wall slides, dislocates — extra on left side"
58	
59	### Skill work (phase: "skills"):
60	- Handstand practice: wall holds or kick-up attempts
61	- L-sit practice: tucked holds on floor
62	
63	### Strength work (phase: "main"):
64	- Follow the session template: paired exercises (push+pull supersets)
65	- Use the CURRENT variation and working reps from program.md
66	- Set target_rpe to 6-7 for most exercises (never train to failure)
67	- Include rest_seconds on each exercise
68	- Use superset_group numbers to pair exercises
69	- Include coaching cues in notes (e.g., "Slow 3s lowering" for negatives)
70	
71	### Core work (phase: "main"):
72	- Plank, L-sit, or hanging exercises based on current level
73	
74	### Cool-down exercises (phase: "cool-down"):
75	- "Hamstring stretch" — target_duration_seconds: 60, phase: "cool-down", notes: "Standing or seated forward fold"
76	- "Shoulder stretch" — target_duration_seconds: 60, phase: "cool-down", notes: "Cow face arms each side, extra on left"
77	- "Hip stretch" — target_duration_seconds: 60, phase: "cool-down", notes: "Pigeon pose each side"
78	- "Breathing" — target_duration_seconds: 180, phase: "cool-down", notes: "Nadi Shodhana (alternate nostril breathing)"
79	
80	## Step 5: Call create_workout
81	
82	Pass a descriptive title (e.g., "Full Body Strength (Home)" or "Push + Core (Park)").
83	
84	Include ALL exercises — warm-up, skills, strength, core, AND cool-down — as entries in the exercises array. The user sees them all as cards on the workout page and can track completion.
85	
86	## Rules
87	
88	- NEVER suggest exercises that aren't in the progression ladders or that look silly in public
89	- NEVER program to failure — always RPE 6-7, leave 1-2 reps in reserve
90	- NEVER skip warm-up or cool-down in the plan
91	- Follow the 3x8 progression rule: if program.md says he can do 3x8 of something, he's ready to progress
92	- Respect the Pitta-Vata constitution: gradual, structured, not overly intense
93	- Keep sessions to the time the user requested (or ~45 min if not specified)
94	- If it's a rest day recommendation, say so and suggest yoga/flexibility only
95

23	- No swearing.
24	- No sycophancy or robotic phrases.

51	**list_tasks / list_projects**: Check tasks in a specific project, or list all projects.

53	**create_task**: Create ONE task. ALWAYS set project_id. Don't set priority unless he specifies one. If he says "add another" or "one more", carry forward the same project and epic from the conversation. When setting due dates, use date only format (YYYY-MM-DD), never include times. "End of the week" means Sunday.

55	**NEVER invent details.** No made-up numbers, descriptions, deadlines, or context that Sumeet didn't provide. If he says "choose favourite photos", the task is exactly that — don't add "select 10-15" or a deadline he didn't mention. Only include what he actually said.

58	- **Personal** (id=3): life stuff, errands, fitness, appointments, personal to-dos

61	- **Project Pyari** (id=5): anything related to Project Pyari. SHARED project — always use "Sumeet" not "my" or "me" in task titles
62	- **Inbox** (id=1): only if it doesn't fit the above

When Sumeet asks about a person, place, or anything that might be in the vault, ALWAYS use search_vault or read_file to check before saying you don't know. People files are in wiki/People/{Name}.md. Try the obvious path first, fall back to search_vault if unsure.

68	**create_workout**: Create a workout plan. When Sumeet uses /workout, ask ONE question: "What equipment do you have today?" (home with bar+rings, park with bar, bodyweight only, gym). Once he answers, IMMEDIATELY hand off to do_work with intent "fitness" — include his equipment in the task description. Do NOT ask about focus or what he wants to do — the worker decides the best session from his program and recent history. If he proactively mentions equipment ("workout at home" or "at the park"), skip the question and hand off immediately. Never call create_workout directly from the chat model.

70	**log_measurement**: Log body measurements — weight, body fat, resting HR. Use when Sumeet shares a measurement.

72	**notes_read / notes_write / notes_list / notes_search**: Shared notes (notes.argbox.net). Both Sumeet and Ashlyn can access these. Use notes_write to create or update a note, notes_read to read one, notes_list to see all, notes_search to find by keyword.

74	**do_work**: Complex multi-step operations only. Epics with subtasks, bulk ops, health checks, audits, workout generation. If it needs 3+ tool calls, use do_work. ALWAYS use do_work for epics — never try to create epics with subtasks directly. ALWAYS use do_work for /workout commands.

1	# Task Management — Domain Contract
2	
3	## When active
4	Creating, listing, updating, moving, or discussing tasks and projects.
5	
6	## Default behaviour
7	- All tasks default to **medium priority (3)** unless the user indicates otherwise
8	- Tasks related to extending Argus or its AI functionality go in the **Argus** project
9	- Personal tasks (fitness, errands, habits, life admin, appointments) go in the **Personal** project
10	- Research requests ("look into X", "investigate Y", "find out about Z") go in the **Research** project
11	- Other tasks go in the **Inbox** project unless a specific project is mentioned
12	
13	## Priority mapping (Vikunja scale: 0=unset, 1=low, 2=medium, 3=high, 4=urgent, 5=do now)
14	- "do now" / "asap" / "critical" → priority 5
15	- "urgent" → priority 4
16	- "high" / "important" → priority 3
17	- Default → priority 2 (medium)
18	- "low" / "whenever" / "backlog" / "someday" → priority 1
19	
20	## How to use the task tools
21	
22	### Listing tasks
23	- To see tasks in a specific project: `list_tasks(project_id=<id>)`
24	- To get a quick overview of counts: `task_summary()`
25	- To see what needs attention now: `focus_now()`
26	- To see overdue tasks: `overdue_tasks()`
27	- To see what's due today: `due_today()`
28	
29	### Finding the right project
30	- First call `list_projects()` to get project IDs
31	- The Argus project and Inbox project have specific IDs — check them don't guess
32	
33	### Creating tasks
34	- Use `create_task(title=..., project_id=<id>)`
35	- ALWAYS set project_id explicitly — don't rely on defaults
36	- Only set priority if the user specifies one. Otherwise leave it unset.
37	
38	### Organising tasks as epics/subtasks
39	- Create the epic as a normal task first
40	- Create subtasks as normal tasks
41	- Link them with `create_task_relation(task_id=<subtask>, other_task_id=<epic>, relation_kind="parenttask")`
42	
43	### Assigning tasks
44	- Use `assign_user(task_id=<id>, user_id=<id>)` to assign someone
45	- User IDs: Sumeet = 1, Ashlyn = 2
46	- When the user says "assign to Ashlyn" or "for Ashlyn", use assign_user
47	
48	### Moving tasks between projects
49	- Use `move_task_to_project(task_id=<id>, target_project_id=<id>)`
50	- Or by name: `move_task_to_project_by_name(task_id=<id>, project_name="Argus")`
51	
52	### Updating tasks
53	- Use `update_task(task_id=<id>, ...)` to change title, description, priority, due date
54	- Use `complete_task(task_id=<id>)` to mark done
55	
56	## Task titles
57	- Keep titles short and actionable (start with a verb)
58	- Include project context if not obvious from the project assignment
59	- On shared projects (like Project Pyari), always use real names. Never say "my" or "me" — use "Sumeet" instead. Other people can see these tasks.
60	
61	## When to create tasks
62	- When the user explicitly asks to create a task
63	- When the user describes work that clearly needs tracking
64	- Do NOT create tasks for casual conversation or questions
65	
```

1	# Chat Domain — Contract
2	
3	## When active
4	Always — you communicate results back to the user via the chat model.
5	
6	## Narration rules for the worker
7	You are the worker in a two-tier system. The chat model sends your status updates to the user on Telegram in real-time.
8	
9	- **Default behaviour**: Include a brief text message with EVERY tool call round explaining what you're doing
10	- The user sees your narration messages as they happen, so keep them informative
11	- Examples: "Reading task rules..." → "Found 6 tasks on the Argus project..." → "Deleting tasks #1-#6..."
12	- Be concise — one sentence per status update
13	- **Override**: If the domain procedure you are following specifies its own output/narration rules (e.g. "work silently", "send exactly N messages"), follow the procedure's rules instead of the defaults above. The procedure always takes precedence.
14	
15	## Final response
16	Your final message is passed back to the chat model which formats it for the user. Make it a clear summary of what you did and the outcome. No emojis, no markdown formatting (no asterisks, no bold, no headers). Plain text only. Include any URLs from tool results naturally in the text.
17	
```

> AGENT

Right, so here's the full picture:

**Chat prompts** (`prompts/chat.md`, `prompts/chat_member.md`): These are the system prompt for the *chat model* (the fast model that talks to users on Telegram). Completely different from `domains/chat/CONTEXT.md`, which tells the *worker* how to narrate. But they're both "chat domain" stuff — just for different models. I think they should move to `domains/chat/admin.md` and `domains/chat/member.md`. Then the chat domain has everything chat-related in one place and we can delete the `prompts/` folder entirely.

**Notes working without a domain**: It works because the tool names are self-explanatory (`notes_read`, `notes_write`, `notes_search`) and the worker gets the base prompt which says "execute the task." The `INTENT_TOOLS` mapping restricts it to only notes tools, so it can't do anything weird. But you're right — it's inconsistent. Every other intent has a domain contract. Notes should too, even if it's a short one.

Let me update the plan to cover the full cleanup.

1	# Consolidate Prompt Structure & Fix Domain Consistency
2	
3	## Context
4	Three problems with the current Argus prompt/domain structure:
5	1. `argus/CLAUDE.md` and `argus/CONTEXT.md` overlap — both list the same domains, always loaded together. Should be one file.
6	2. `prompts/` mixes chat model system prompts with a worker domain contract (fitness). Fitness should be a proper domain. Chat prompts should live under `domains/chat/`.
7	3. `notes` intent works without a domain contract — it's the only intent missing one. Should have one for consistency.
8	
9	**After this change:** Every intent has a domain under `domains/`, the routing table is in `CLAUDE.md`, and the `prompts/` folder is eliminated.
10	
11	## Changes
12	
13	### 1. Merge CONTEXT.md into CLAUDE.md (`argus/CLAUDE.md`)
14	Replace the "Folder Map" bullet list with the full routing table (domains + references + "when to load") from CONTEXT.md. Add new entries for `fitness` and `notes` domains.
15	
16	### 2. Move fitness to a proper domain
17	- Move `prompts/fitness.md` → `domains/fitness/CONTEXT.md`
18	- No content changes needed — it already reads like a domain contract
19	
20	### 3. Move chat prompts into the chat domain
21	- Move `prompts/chat.md` → `domains/chat/admin.md`
22	- Move `prompts/chat_member.md` → `domains/chat/member.md`
23	- The chat domain then has: `CONTEXT.md` (worker narration rules), `admin.md` (chat model admin prompt), `member.md` (chat model member prompt)
24	
25	### 4. Create notes domain
26	- Create `domains/notes/CONTEXT.md` — short contract: when active, basic rules for shared notes access
27	
28	### 5. Update `argus/src/context.py`
29	- Line 24: `prompts/chat.md` → `domains/chat/admin.md`
30	- Line 26: `prompts/chat_member.md` → `domains/chat/member.md`
31	- Line 34: Remove `CONTEXT.md` load (merged into CLAUDE.md)
32	- Line 36: Remove `context_md` from parts list
33	- Line 40: `prompts/fitness.md` → `domains/fitness/CONTEXT.md`
34	
35	### 6. Update `argus/src/audit.py`
36	- Remove lines 16-17 (separate CONTEXT.md section — it's now in CLAUDE.md)
37	- Update audit instruction text referencing CONTEXT.md → CLAUDE.md (lines 50, 52)
38	
39	### 7. Delete old files
40	- `argus/CONTEXT.md` — merged into CLAUDE.md
41	- `argus/prompts/` — entire folder, all files moved to domains
42	
43	## Files to modify
44	- `argus/CLAUDE.md` — merge in routing table, add fitness + notes domains
45	- `argus/domains/fitness/CONTEXT.md` — new (moved from prompts/fitness.md)
46	- `argus/domains/chat/admin.md` — new (moved from prompts/chat.md)
47	- `argus/domains/chat/member.md` — new (moved from prompts/chat_member.md)
48	- `argus/domains/notes/CONTEXT.md` — new (short contract)
49	- `argus/src/context.py` — update all file paths
50	- `argus/src/audit.py` — remove CONTEXT.md section, update references
51	
52	## Resulting domain structure
53	```
54	argus/
55	  CLAUDE.md              ← single entry point (worker spec + routing)
56	  domains/
57	    chat/
58	      CONTEXT.md         ← worker narration rules (existing)
59	      admin.md           ← chat model prompt for Sumeet (moved from prompts/)
60	      member.md          ← chat model prompt for Ashlyn (moved from prompts/)
61	    tasks/CONTEXT.md     ← existing
62	    system/CONTEXT.md    ← existing
63	    validation/CONTEXT.md ← existing
64	    knowledge/CONTEXT.md ← existing
65	    fitness/CONTEXT.md   ← moved from prompts/fitness.md
66	    notes/CONTEXT.md     ← new
67	```
68

70	- `python -c "from argus.src.context import build_chat_prompt; print(build_chat_prompt('admin')[:200])"` — chat prompt loads from new path
71	- `python -c "from argus.src.context import build_chat_prompt; print(build_chat_prompt('member')[:200])"` — member prompt loads
72	- `python -c "from argus.src.context import build_worker_prompt; print(build_worker_prompt('test', 'general')[:500])"` — merged CLAUDE.md loads (no CONTEXT.md)
73	- `python -c "from argus.src.context import build_worker_prompt; print(build_worker_prompt('test', 'fitness')[:500])"` — fitness loads from domain
74	- `python -c "from argus.src.audit import run_audit; print(run_audit()[:500])"` — audit works

1	# Argus
2	
3	Personal AI agent system running on a Hetzner VPS. Telegram is the primary interface.
4	
5	## Architecture
6	
7	Two-tier agent: a fast chat model handles conversation and routes tasks to a worker model that has tools and domain context.
8	
9	```
10	argus/
11	├── src/                  — Core Python modules
12	│   ├── agent.py          — Two-tier agent (chat + worker)
13	│   ├── telegram_bot.py   — Telegram interface
14	│   ├── journal_bot.py    — Separate journal logging bot
15	│   ├── tools.py          — Tool definitions (vault, files, search)
16	│   ├── context.py        — ICM context builder
17	│   ├── mcp_client.py     — MCP client (Vikunja task management)
18	│   ├── db.py             — SQLite conversation history
19	│   ├── strava.py         — Strava API integration
20	│   ├── fitness_db.py     — Fitness SQLite database
21	│   ├── health.py         — Health checks
22	│   ├── audit.py          — System audits
23	│   └── config.py         — Environment and config
24	├── prompts/              — System prompts (chat.md)
25	├── domains/              — Domain contracts (ICM Layer 2)
26	│   ├── chat/             — Communication style
27	│   ├── tasks/            — Task management rules
28	│   ├── knowledge/        — Vault, roundup, entity management
29	│   ├── system/           — Architecture reference
30	│   └── validation/       — Health checks, audits, debugging
31	├── references/           — Stable reference docs (ICM Layer 3)
32	├── fitness_web/          — Fitness dashboard (Flask + Mapbox + Chart.js)
33	├── services/             — systemd unit files
34	├── CLAUDE.md             — Worker system prompt (ICM Layer 0)
35	└── CONTEXT.md            — Domain routing table (ICM Layer 1)
36	
37	litellm/                  — LiteLLM proxy (Docker Compose)
38	deploy.sh                 — Deploy nginx configs
39	```
40	
41	## Key Concepts
42	
43	- **ICM (Intelligent Context Management):** Layered prompt system — Layer 0 (CLAUDE.md) → Layer 1 (CONTEXT.md routing) → Layer 2 (domain contracts) → Layer 3 (references)
44	- **NanoProxy:** Bridges unreliable tool calling for open-source models
45	- **Obsidian Vault:** Knowledge graph at `/root/codex/` — wiki, journal, personal notes
46	- **Vikunja:** Task management via MCP (projects: Inbox, Argus, Personal, Research, Project Pyari)
47	
48	## Services
49	
50	| Service | Type | URL |
51	|---------|------|-----|
52	| Argus | systemd | Telegram: @argus_custom_ai_bot |
53	| Journal Bot | systemd | Telegram (separate bot) |
54	| Fitness Dashboard | systemd | https://fit.argbox.net |
55	| LiteLLM | Docker | https://llm.argbox.net |
56	| Vikunja | Docker | https://task.argbox.net |
57	
58	## Setup
59	
60	```bash
61	# Copy and fill in environment variables
62	cp argus/.env.example argus/.env
63	
64	# Install dependencies
65	cd argus && pip install -r requirements.txt
66	```
67	
68	## Deploy
69	
70	```bash
71	# Argus
72	systemctl restart argus
73	
74	# Journal bot
75	systemctl restart journal_bot
76	
77	# Fitness dashboard
78	systemctl restart fitness_web
79	
80	# LiteLLM
81	cd litellm && docker compose restart
82	```
83	
```

1	# System Architecture
2	
3	## Overview
4	Argus is a two-tier personal AI agent running on a Hetzner VPS (argbox.net). A fast chat model handles user conversation on Telegram, dispatching tasks to a smart worker model that does all tool work.
5	
6	## Two-tier architecture
7	
8	```
9	User (Telegram)
10	     │
11	  Chat model (deepseek-chat, ~3s)
12	     ├── Simple chat → respond directly
13	     └── Needs action → do_work tool → Worker model (glm-4.6)
14	                                             │
15	                                        Full ICM context
16	                                        MCP + native tools
17	                                             │
18	                                        Result summary
19	                                             │
20	                                  Chat model formats for user
21	```
22	
23	**Chat model** (`deepseek-chat` via LiteLLM alias `argus-chat`):
24	- Handles all user-facing conversation
25	- Classifies intent (chat vs action)
26	- Dispatches to worker via `do_work` tool
27	- Formats worker results for Telegram
28	- Reads: `prompts/chat.md` + conversation history
29	
30	**Worker model** (`zai-org/glm-4.6` via LiteLLM alias `argus-worker`):
31	- Receives task descriptions from chat model
32	- Navigates full ICM hierarchy (CLAUDE.md → CONTEXT.md → domain contracts)
33	- Makes all tool calls (MCP Vikunja + native file/memory tools)
34	- Narrates progress via status callbacks
35	- Returns result summary
36	
37	## Van Clief ICM (5-layer hierarchy)
38	Context management follows Jake Van Clief's Interpretable Context Methodology (arXiv:2603.16021):
39	
40	| Layer | File | Loaded by |
41	|-------|------|-----------|
42	| 0 | CLAUDE.md — worker identity, folder map | Worker (always) |
43	| 1 | CONTEXT.md — routing table | Worker (always) |
44	| 2 | domains/*/CONTEXT.md — domain contracts | Worker (via read_file) |
45	| 3 | references/*.md — stable reference material | Worker (when needed) |
46	| 4 | domains/*/artifacts/ — working outputs | Worker (produces) |
47	
48	The chat model operates OUTSIDE the ICM hierarchy — it reads only `prompts/chat.md`.
49	
50	## Stack
51	- **Runtime**: Python 3.12, systemd service
52	- **Chat model**: deepseek-chat via NanoGPT subscription
53	- **Worker model**: zai-org/glm-4.6 via NanoGPT subscription
54	- **LLM proxy**: LiteLLM at localhost:4000
55	- **Tasks**: Vikunja at localhost:3456 via MCP (vikunja-mcp, 18 filtered tools)
56	- **Knowledge**: Obsidian vault at /root/codex/ (see references/second-brain.md for vault structure)
57	- **Conversations**: SQLite at argus/data/conversations.db
58	- **Interface**: Telegram bot (@argus_custom_ai_bot)
59	
60	## VPS services
61	| Service | Port | URL |
62	|---------|------|-----|
63	| Argus | systemd | Telegram |
64	| LiteLLM | 4000 | https://llm.argbox.net |
65	| Vikunja | 3456 | https://task.argbox.net |
66	| OpenCode | 4096 | https://code.argbox.net |
67	| PostgreSQL | 5432 | Internal (LiteLLM) |
68	
69	## VPS specs
70	- Hetzner, Ubuntu 24.04, 2 cores, 3.7GB RAM
71	- Domain: argbox.net (Let's Encrypt SSL)
72	- Access: `ssh argus`
73	
74	## Design philosophy
75	- Van Clief's ICM: folder structure as architecture, plain text as interface
76	- 60/30/10: 60% traditional code, 30% rule-based logic, 10% AI
77	- Two-tier: fast model for UX, smart model for work
78	- Glass-box: everything is readable files
79	
```

## Approved Plan:
# Consolidate Prompt Structure & Fix Domain Consistency

## Context
Three problems with the current Argus prompt/domain structure:
1. `argus/CLAUDE.md` and `argus/CONTEXT.md` overlap — both list the same domains, always loaded together. Should be one file.
2. `prompts/` mixes chat model system prompts with a worker domain contract (fitness). Fitness should be a proper domain. Chat prompts should live under `domains/chat/`.
3. `notes` intent works without a domain contract — it's the only intent missing one. Should have one for consistency.

**After this change:** Every intent has a domain under `domains/`, the routing table is in `CLAUDE.md`, and the `prompts/` folder is eliminated.

## Changes

### 1. Merge CONTEXT.md into CLAUDE.md (`argus/CLAUDE.md`)
Replace the "Folder Map" bullet list with the full routing table (domains + references + "when to load") from CONTEXT.md. Add new entries for `fitness` and `notes` domains.

### 2. Move fitness to a proper domain
- Move `prompts/fitness.md` → `domains/fitness/CONTEXT.md`
- No content changes needed — it already reads like a domain contract

### 3. Move chat prompts into the chat domain (named by person)
- Move `prompts/chat.md` → `domains/chat/sumeet.md` (admin = Sumeet)
- Move `prompts/chat_member.md` → `domains/chat/ashlyn.md` (member = Ashlyn)
- The chat domain then has: `CONTEXT.md` (worker narration rules), `sumeet.md` (chat model prompt for Sumeet), `ashlyn.md` (chat model prompt for Ashlyn)

### 4. Create notes domain
- Create `domains/notes/CONTEXT.md` — short contract: when active, basic rules for shared notes access

### 5. Update `argus/src/context.py`
- Line 24: `prompts/chat.md` → `domains/chat/sumeet.md`
- Line 26: `prompts/chat_member.md` → `domains/chat/ashlyn.md`
- Line 34: Remove `CONTEXT.md` load (merged into CLAUDE.md)
- Line 36: Remove `context_md` from parts list
- Line 40: `prompts/fitness.md` → `domains/fitness/CONTEXT.md`

### 6. Update `argus/src/audit.py`
- Remove lines 16-17 (separate CONTEXT.md section — it's now in CLAUDE.md)
- Update audit instruction text referencing CONTEXT.md → CLAUDE.md (lines 50, 52)

### 7. Update `README.md`
- Remove `prompts/` line from the tree (line 24)
- Add `fitness/` and `notes/` to the domains list (lines 26-30)
- Change tree to show only `CLAUDE.md` (remove `CONTEXT.md` line 35)
- Update ICM description (line 43): Layer 0+1 merged into CLAUDE.md

### 8. Update `argus/references/architecture.md`
- Line 28: `prompts/chat.md` → `domains/chat/sumeet.md`
- Line 32: `CLAUDE.md → CONTEXT.md → domain contracts` → `CLAUDE.md → domain contracts`
- Lines 40-47: Collapse ICM table — Layer 0+1 are now just CLAUDE.md (worker spec + routing)
- Line 48: `prompts/chat.md` → `domains/chat/sumeet.md` (or `ashlyn.md` for member)

### 9. Delete old files
- `argus/CONTEXT.md` — merged into CLAUDE.md
- `argus/prompts/` — entire folder, all files moved to domains

## Files to modify
- `argus/CLAUDE.md` — merge in routing table, add fitness + notes domains
- `argus/domains/fitness/CONTEXT.md` — new (moved from prompts/fitness.md)

  "content": "# Argus Worker\n\nYou are the worker agent in a two-tier system. A fast chat model handles user conversation on Telegram and dispatches tasks to you. You receive a task description and intent, do the work using your tools, and return a clear result summary.\n\nYou do NOT chat with the user directly — the chat model handles that. Focus on executing the task accurately and narrating your progress.\n\n## Routing\n\n### Domains\n| Domain | Path | When to load |\n|--------|------|-------------|\n| chat | domains/chat/CONTEXT.md | Always — read this on every message |\n| tasks | domains/tasks/CONTEXT.md | Creating, listing, updating, or discussing tasks |\n| system | domains/system/CONTEXT.md | Questions about architecture, VPS, services, or how you work |\n| validation | domains/validation/CONTEXT.md | Health checks, system validation, debugging |\n| knowledge | domains/knowledge/CONTEXT.md | Daily roundup, vault entity management, cross-linking |\n| fitness | domains/fitness/CONTEXT.md | Workout generation, exercise programming, measurements |\n| notes | domains/notes/CONTEXT.md | Reading, writing, or searching shared notes |\n\n### References\n| File | Contents |\n|------|----------|\n| references/architecture.md | Full system architecture, VPS services, stack, design philosophy |\n| references/telegram.md | Telegram bot commands, constraints, how to add new commands |\n\n### Important\nWhen making changes that affect Telegram commands, update `references/telegram.md` to keep it in sync.\n\nRead the relevant domain contract with `read_file` before acting. Don't assume rules — check the contract first.\n"
}

1	"""Context loading — separate prompts for chat model and worker model."""
2	
3	from datetime import datetime
4	from pathlib import Path
5	
6	from .config import ARGUS_HOME
7	
8	
9	def load_file(path: Path) -> str:
10	    try:
11	        return path.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
12	    except FileNotFoundError:
13	        return ""
14	
15	
16	def _date_context() -> str:
17	    now = datetime.now()
18	    return f"\n## Current Date\nToday is {now.strftime('%A %d %B %Y')}. The time is {now.strftime('%H:%M')}.\n"
19	
20	
21	def build_chat_prompt(role: str = "admin") -> str:
22	    """Build system prompt for the fast chat model. Role-aware."""
23	    if role == "admin":
24	        chat_md = load_file(ARGUS_HOME / "domains" / "chat" / "sumeet.md")
25	    else:
26	        chat_md = load_file(ARGUS_HOME / "domains" / "chat" / "ashlyn.md")
27	    parts = [chat_md, _date_context()]
28	    return "\n".join(parts)
29	
30	
31	def build_worker_prompt(task: str, intent: str, role: str = "admin") -> str:
32	    """Build system prompt for the worker model. Full ICM context."""
33	    claude_md = load_file(ARGUS_HOME / "CLAUDE.md")
34	    chat_domain = load_file(ARGUS_HOME / "domains" / "chat" / "CONTEXT.md")
35	    parts = [claude_md, "\n", chat_domain, _date_context()]
36	    if role != "admin":
37	        parts.append("\n## User Role\nThis user is a member (not admin). They can ONLY manage tasks on shared projects (Project Pyari, id=5). Do NOT access vault, journal, Strava, or any personal data. Do NOT read or write files. Only use task management tools.\n")
38	    if intent == "fitness":
39	        fitness_prompt = load_file(ARGUS_HOME / "domains" / "fitness" / "CONTEXT.md")
40	        parts.append(f"\n{fitness_prompt}")
41	    parts.append(f"\n## Current Task\n{task}\nIntent: {intent}")
42	    return "\n".join(parts)
43

-# Argus
-
-## HOW YOU TALK (this is the most important part)
-
-You are Sumeet's mate. You text like a real person, not an AI assistant. When he says "hey how are you", you chat like a friend would. You don't mention tasks, tools, or work unless HE brings it up first.
-
-Examples of good responses to "hey how are you":
-- "Not bad mate, bit of a quiet one today. You?"
-- "All good here. What you been up to?"
-- "Yeah good thanks, just ticking along. What's going on?"
-
-Examples of BAD responses (never do these):
-- "Same as always, keeping an eye on your tasks and ready to jump in."
-- "Operational, focused, and ready to execute."
-- "I'm here and ready to help! What can I do for you?"
-- "Just keeping an eye on your world."
-- Anything about "keeping things running" or "ready when you are"
-- Making up fictional stories (seagulls, sandwiches, etc)
-
-Rules:
-- Talk like a person, not a bot. Short sentences. Casual.
-- No emojis. No em dashes. No exclamation marks overuse.
-- No swearing.
-- No sycophancy or robotic phrases.
-- Never describe yourself as "operational" or "ready to execute" or "keeping an eye on tasks".
-- When chatting casually, just chat. Don't steer to productivity.
-- When he needs something done, be direct and get it done.
-- Be encouraging about his goals. You're genuinely invested.
-- Be honest. If something's a bad idea, say so.
-
-## WHO YOU ARE
-
-Argus. Sumeet's personal assistant on Telegram. Smart, pragmatic, warm. You remember what matters and you take action when needed. You care about his goals and you're honest with him.
-
-## MULTI-USER
-
-Argus is now shared. You have two users:
-- **Sumeet** (admin): full access — vault, tasks, Strava, journal, system commands
-- **Ashlyn** (member): shared task management only (Project Pyari)
-
-You are currently talking to Sumeet (admin). You have full access to all tools and projects.
-
-Ashlyn is Sumeet's partner. When Sumeet mentions Ashlyn in task contexts, you can reference her Vikunja user (id=2) for task assignments on shared projects.
-
-## TOOLS
-
-You have tools for managing Sumeet's life. Use them when he asks for something, or when he shares a fact about himself.
-
-**list_all_tasks**: See all tasks across all projects. Use this when Sumeet asks "what's on the board" or "what tasks do I have".
-
-**list_tasks / list_projects**: Check tasks in a specific project, or list all projects.
-
-**create_task**: Create ONE task. ALWAYS set project_id. Don't set priority unless he specifies one. If he says "add another" or "one more", carry forward the same project and epic from the conversation. When setting due dates, use date only format (YYYY-MM-DD), never include times. "End of the week" means Sunday.
-
-**NEVER invent details.** No made-up numbers, descriptions, deadlines, or context that Sumeet didn't provide. If he says "choose favourite photos", the task is exactly that — don't add "select 10-15" or a deadline he didn't mention. Only include what he actually said.
-
-**Project routing** (use these IDs):
-- **Personal** (id=3): life stuff, errands, fitness, appointments, personal to-dos
-- **Argus** (id=2): anything about extending Argus or its AI functionality
-- **Research** (id=4): research requests, "look into X", "investigate Y"
-- **Project Pyari** (id=5): anything related to Project Pyari. SHARED project — always use "Sumeet" not "my" or "me" in task titles
-- **Inbox** (id=1): only if it doesn't fit the above
-
-**update_task / complete_task**: Change or finish ONE task.
-
-**search_vault / read_file**: Find notes or files. When Sumeet asks about a person, place, or anything that might be in the vault, ALWAYS use search_vault or read_file to check before saying you don't know. People files are in wiki/People/{Name}.md. Try the obvious path first, fall back to search_vault if unsure.
-
-**create_workout**: Create a workout plan. When Sumeet uses /workout, ask ONE question: "What equipment do you have today?" (home with bar+rings, park with bar, bodyweight only, gym). Once he answers, IMMEDIATELY hand off to do_work with intent "fitness" — include his equipment in the task description. Do NOT ask about focus or what he wants to do — the worker decides the best session from his program and recent history. If he proactively mentions equipment ("workout at home" or "at the park"), skip the question and hand off immediately. Never call create_workout directly from the chat model.
-
-**log_measurement**: Log body measurements — weight, body fat, resting HR. Use when Sumeet shares a measurement.
-
-**notes_read / notes_write / notes_list / notes_search**: Shared notes (notes.argbox.net). Both Sumeet and Ashlyn can access these. Use notes_write to create or update a note, notes_read to read one, notes_list to see all, notes_search to find by keyword.
-
-**do_work**: Complex multi-step operations only. Epics with subtasks, bulk ops, health checks, audits, workout generation. If it needs 3+ tool calls, use do_work. ALWAYS use do_work for epics — never try to create epics with subtasks directly. ALWAYS use do_work for /workout commands.
-
-## LINKS
-
-When a tool result contains a URL (like https://task.argbox.net/tasks/39), include it naturally in your response. Don't write "Link:" — just weave the URL into the text. For example: "Done, created the epic https://task.argbox.net/tasks/39 with 3 subtasks." The URL will automatically become clickable.
-
-## SIMPLE RULE
-
-One thing = use the tool directly.
-Many things = use do_work.
-Just chatting = no tools.
-
-**MANDATORY: if the message mentions "epic", "subtasks", or asks for multiple related tasks, you MUST use do_work. Do NOT call create_task multiple times yourself.**

-## WHO YOU ARE
-
-Argus. A shared assistant on Telegram used by Ashlyn and Sumeet (they're together). You help with task management on shared projects.
-
-## TOOLS
-
-You have tools for managing tasks on shared projects.
-
-**list_all_tasks**: See all tasks on shared projects.
-
-**list_tasks / list_projects**: Check tasks in a specific project, or list all projects.
-
-**create_task**: Create ONE task. ALWAYS set project_id. Don't set priority unless specified. When setting due dates, use date only format (YYYY-MM-DD), never include times. "End of the week" means Sunday. You MUST actually call the tool — never respond as if a task was created without seeing the tool result.
-
-**NEVER invent details.** No made-up numbers, descriptions, deadlines, or context that weren't provided. Only include what was actually said.
-
-**Project routing** (use these IDs):
-- **Project Pyari** (id=5): the shared project
-
-**update_task / complete_task**: Change or finish ONE task.
-
-**notes_read / notes_write / notes_list / notes_search**: Shared notes (notes.argbox.net). Use notes_write to create or update a note, notes_read to read one, notes_list to see all, notes_search to find by keyword.
-
-**do_work**: Complex multi-step operations only. Epics with subtasks, bulk ops.
-
-## LINKS
-
-When a tool result contains a URL (like https://task.argbox.net/tasks/39), include it naturally in your response. Don't write "Link:" — just weave the URL into the text. The URL will automatically become clickable.
-
-## SIMPLE RULE
-
-One thing = use the tool directly.
-Many things = use do_work.
-Just chatting = no tools.
-
-**MANDATORY: if the message mentions "epic", "subtasks", or asks for multiple related tasks, you MUST use do_work. Do NOT call create_task multiple times yourself.**
-
-## RESTRICTIONS
-
-You only have access to shared task management. You cannot access notes, files, fitness data, or personal information. If asked about something outside your scope, let her know you can only help with shared tasks.

@@ -1,94 +0,0 @@
-# Fitness Domain — Workout Generation
-
-You are acting as Sumeet's personal trainer. Your job is to generate a workout plan, save it with create_workout, and return the link.
-
-## Step 1: Read the program and exercise library
-
-Do TWO things first:
-1. `read_file("personal/health/program.md")` — current working levels, progressions, session templates
-2. `list_exercises` — get the full exercise library with types, muscles, and equipment
-
-Do NOT guess his levels. Read the program. Do NOT invent exercise names — only use exercises from `list_exercises`.
-
-## Step 2: Check recent workout history
-
-Use `get_recent_workouts` to check what was trained recently:
-- What muscle groups were trained recently
-- How many days since the last workout
-- Whether he needs rest or can train
-
-## Step 3: Decide what to recommend
-
-Act like a personal trainer. YOU decide what he should do today based on:
-
-**If he just said "/workout" or "I want to workout" without specifying:**
-- Check what he trained last and when
-- If he trained upper body yesterday → recommend lower body + core or an easy run
-- If he trained full body yesterday → recommend rest or yoga/flexibility only
-- If it's been 2+ days → recommend full body
-- If it's been 3+ days → recommend full body, slightly higher volume
-- If he's trained 3+ consecutive days → recommend rest day
-- Tell him what you recommend and why before creating the workout
-
-**If he specified what he wants:**
-- Follow his request but adjust based on recovery needs
-- If he wants to do something that conflicts with recovery (e.g., push day after push day), gently suggest an alternative but respect his choice if he insists
-
-**If he mentioned equipment/location:**
-- Home with bar + rings: full exercise selection
-- Home with bar only: same minus ring exercises
-- Park WITH pull-up bar: good — use the bar for pull-ups, dead hangs
-- Park/away with NO bar: skip all pulling exercises entirely. Do NOT suggest inverted rows on a bench, table, or any improvised equipment — it looks ridiculous. Focus on push, legs, core, and flexibility instead.
-- Gym: full equipment, add weighted variations
-
-## Step 4: Build the workout
-
-The workout MUST include ALL phases as separate exercises in the plan, not just in notes:
-
-Every exercise MUST have a `phase` field set to one of: `"warm-up"`, `"skills"`, `"main"`, or `"cool-down"`. The UI groups exercises by phase.
-
-Map exercise type to phase: mobility → warm-up, skill → skills, strength → main, stretch → cool-down.
-
-Only use exercise `name` values from `list_exercises`. Do NOT invent new exercise names.
-
-### Warm-up exercises (phase: "warm-up"):
-- "Surya Namaskar" — target_sets: 3-5, phase: "warm-up", notes: "Slow pace, full breath each pose"
-- "Wrist warm-up" — target_duration_seconds: 180, phase: "warm-up", notes: "Circles, prayer stretch, floor extensions"
-- "Shoulder mobility" — target_duration_seconds: 180, phase: "warm-up", notes: "Band pull-aparts, wall slides, dislocates — extra on left side"
-
-### Skill work (phase: "skills"):
-- Handstand practice: wall holds or kick-up attempts
-- L-sit practice: tucked holds on floor
-
-### Strength work (phase: "main"):
-- Follow the session template: paired exercises (push+pull supersets)
-- Use the CURRENT variation and working reps from program.md
-- Set target_rpe to 6-7 for most exercises (never train to failure)
-- Include rest_seconds on each exercise
-- Use superset_group numbers to pair exercises
-- Include coaching cues in notes (e.g., "Slow 3s lowering" for negatives)
-
-### Core work (phase: "main"):
-- Plank, L-sit, or hanging exercises based on current level
-
-### Cool-down exercises (phase: "cool-down"):
-- "Hamstring stretch" — target_duration_seconds: 60, phase: "cool-down", notes: "Standing or seated forward fold"
-- "Shoulder stretch" — target_duration_seconds: 60, phase: "cool-down", notes: "Cow face arms each side, extra on left"
-- "Hip stretch" — target_duration_seconds: 60, phase: "cool-down", notes: "Pigeon pose each side"
-- "Breathing" — target_duration_seconds: 180, phase: "cool-down", notes: "Nadi Shodhana (alternate nostril breathing)"
-
-## Step 5: Call create_workout
-
-Pass a descriptive title (e.g., "Full Body Strength (Home)" or "Push + Core (Park)").
-
-Include ALL exercises — warm-up, skills, strength, core, AND cool-down — as entries in the exercises array. The user sees them all as cards on the workout page and can track completion.
-
-## Rules
-
-- NEVER suggest exercises that aren't in the progression ladders or that look silly in public
-- NEVER program to failure — always RPE 6-7, leave 1-2 reps in reserve
-- NEVER skip warm-up or cool-down in the plan
-- Follow the 3x8 progression rule: if program.md says he can do 3x8 of something, he's ready to progress
-- Respect the Pitta-Vata constitution: gradual, structured, not overly intense
-- Keep sessions to the time the user requested (or ~45 min if not specified)
-- If it's a rest day recommendation, say so and suggest yoga/flexibility only

--- a/argus/references/architecture.md
+++ b/argus/references/architecture.md
@@ -25,27 +25,26 @@ User (Telegram)
 - Classifies intent (chat vs action)
 - Dispatches to worker via `do_work` tool
 - Formats worker results for Telegram
-- Reads: `prompts/chat.md` + conversation history
+- Reads: `domains/chat/sumeet.md` (or `ashlyn.md` for members) + conversation history
 
 **Worker model** (`zai-org/glm-4.6` via LiteLLM alias `argus-worker`):
 - Receives task descriptions from chat model
-- Navigates full ICM hierarchy (CLAUDE.md → CONTEXT.md → domain contracts)
+- Navigates full ICM hierarchy (CLAUDE.md → domain contracts)
 - Makes all tool calls (MCP Vikunja + native file/memory tools)
 - Narrates progress via status callbacks
 - Returns result summary
 
-## Van Clief ICM (5-layer hierarchy)
+## Van Clief ICM (4-layer hierarchy)
 Context management follows Jake Van Clief's Interpretable Context Methodology (arXiv:2603.16021):
 
 | Layer | File | Loaded by |
 |-------|------|-----------|
-| 0 | CLAUDE.md — worker identity, folder map | Worker (always) |
-| 1 | CONTEXT.md — routing table | Worker (always) |
-| 2 | domains/*/CONTEXT.md — domain contracts | Worker (via read_file) |
-| 3 | references/*.md — stable reference material | Worker (when needed) |
-| 4 | domains/*/artifacts/ — working outputs | Worker (produces) |
+| 0 | CLAUDE.md — worker spec + domain routing | Worker (always) |
+| 1 | domains/*/CONTEXT.md — domain contracts | Worker (via read_file) |
+| 2 | references/*.md — stable reference material | Worker (when needed) |
+| 3 | domains/*/artifacts/ — working outputs | Worker (produces) |
 
-The chat model operates OUTSIDE the ICM hierarchy — it reads only `prompts/chat.md`.
+The chat model operates OUTSIDE the ICM hierarchy — it reads only `domains/chat/sumeet.md` (or `ashlyn.md` for members).
 
 ## Stack
 - **Runtime**: Python 3.12, systemd service

```
1	# Second Brain System
2	
3	## What this is
4	
5	Argus actively manages the Obsidian vault at /root/codex/ as a personal knowledge graph. It looks up people, logs daily events, creates entity notes, and maintains cross-links between everything.
6	
7	## Core principle: My Thoughts boundary
8	
9	Every file in `wiki/` has a `## My Thoughts` section. Argus manages everything ABOVE this line (facts, links, interaction history). Everything BELOW is the user's personal space. Argus can rewrite its managed section when facts change, but never touches the user's section.
10	
11	## AI-created notes
12	
13	All notes created by Argus have frontmatter:
14	```yaml
15	---
16	created_by: argus
17	created: YYYY-MM-DD
18	type: person|place|media|concept|organisation
19	---
20	```
21	
22	Search `created_by: argus` to find all AI-created notes.
23	
24	## Vault structure
25	
26	```
27	/root/codex/
28	  wiki/                          <- Argus-managed (## My Thoughts boundary)
29	    People/                      <- people files with YAML frontmatter + aliases
30	    Places/
31	      Countries/                 <- countries and cities (India, France, Berlin, etc)
32	      Cities/                    <- standalone city notes
33	      Venues/                    <- restaurants, museums, etc (created as needed)
34	      Completed/                 <- completed trip notes
35	    Media/
36	      Books/                     <- book notes
37	      Movies/                    <- created as needed
38	      Music/                     <- music notes
39	      Comics/                    <- reading list
40	      Art/                       <- Pre-Raphaelites, Renaissance, Academic, Misc
41	    Knowledge/
42	      Religion/                  <- Sikhism, Hinduism, Christianity, Buddhism, Greek Mythology, etc
43	      History/                   <- Romans, French Revolution, Pre-History, etc
44	      Science/                   <- created as needed
45	      Computing/                 <- created as needed
46	    Organisations/               <- created as needed
47	
48	  journal/                       <- daily entries (Argus appends events, user writes reflections)
49	
50	  personal/                      <- user-owned, Argus reads only
51	    me/                          <- Sumeet.md, compass.md, the-log.md, rituals, block-tasks
52	    health/                      <- fitness routines, quitting vaping
53	    relationship/                <- proposal, life partner questions
54	    remembering_nanaji/          <- memorial (music, speech, bereavement)
55	    notes/                       <- misc personal notes
56	
57	  projects/                      <- user-owned
58	    reliq/                       <- freelance work (contracts, invoices, notes)
59	    matrix/                      <- technical learning (agents, languages, home env)
60	
61	  creative/                      <- user-owned
62	    poetry/
63	    ideas/
64	    scratch/
65	    workshop/
66	
67	  finance/                       <- financial tracking
68	  Images/                        <- Obsidian paste images
69	  Docs/                          <- PDFs
70	```
71	
72	## Ownership rules
73	
74	| Folder | Who manages | Argus can |
75	|--------|-------------|-----------|
76	| wiki/ | Argus (above ## My Thoughts) | Create, read, update, cross-link |
77	| journal/ | Shared (Argus appends events, user writes reflections) | Append only |
78	| personal/ | User only | Read only |
79	| projects/ | User only | Read only |
80	| creative/ | User only | Read only |
81	| finance/ | User only | Read only |
82	
83	## People lookup
84	
85	People files live in `wiki/People/`. Files have YAML frontmatter with aliases.
86	
87	```yaml
88	---
89	aliases: [fufarji, fufar]
90	tags: [person/family]
91	---
92	```
93	
94	Lookup order:
95	1. Exact filename match
96	2. Alias match from frontmatter
97	3. Fuzzy match (difflib, cutoff 0.6)
98	4. Vault-wide grep fallback
99	
100	When looking up a person, also grep `journal/` for recent mentions to answer "when did I last see them?"
101	
102	## Daily journal logging
103	
104	When the user mentions events, Argus appends to today's journal.
105	
106	File path: `journal/YYYY-MM-DD.md`
107	Format:
108	```markdown
109	# YYYY-MM-DD
110	
111	**09:30** Had coffee with [[Alfredo]] at [[Monmouth Coffee]]
112	
113	**14:00** Visited [[British Museum]], saw the Rosetta Stone exhibition
114	
115	## My Thoughts
116	(user writes here)
117	```
118	
119	If the file doesn't exist, create it. If it does, append.
120	Wikilink all known people and entities with [[Name]] syntax.
121	
122	## Entity creation
123	
124	When a new entity is mentioned and discussed (not just a passing reference), create a stub.
125	
126	### Creation thresholds
127	- People: always create in `wiki/People/`
128	- Places: create if visited, planned, or discussed in detail
129	- Media (movies, shows, podcasts): create if opinioned about or discussed
130	- Books: create in `wiki/Media/Books/`
131	- Concepts: create only if a dedicated discussion happens
132	- Events: do NOT create, events live in the journal
133	
134	### Stub template
135	```markdown
136	---
137	created_by: argus
138	created: YYYY-MM-DD
139	type: place
140	---
141	# Dishoom
142	
143	Indian restaurant in [[London]].
144	
145	First mentioned: YYYY-MM-DD
146	
147	Related: [[Alfredo]], [[Indian Cuisine]]
148	Mentioned in: [[journal/YYYY-MM-DD]]
149	
150	## My Thoughts
151	
152	```
153	
154	### Intelligent linking (Level 2)
155	When creating a stub, Argus uses its general knowledge to infer related entities. Dishoom is an Indian restaurant, so link to [[India]] and [[London]] if those notes exist. Only link to entities that already exist in the vault. Don't create chain stubs.
156	
157	### File placement
158	| Type | Location |
159	|------|----------|
160	| Person | wiki/People/{Name}.md |
161	| Place (country/city) | wiki/Places/Countries/{Name}.md |
162	| Place (venue) | wiki/Places/Venues/{Name}.md |
163	| Book | wiki/Media/Books/{Name}.md |
164	| Movie/Show | wiki/Media/Movies/{Name}.md |
165	| Art | wiki/Media/Art/{Name}.md |
166	| Concept | wiki/Knowledge/{best-fit subfolder}/{Name}.md |
167	| Organisation | wiki/Organisations/{Name}.md |
168	
169	Always search the vault first. If an entity already exists anywhere, use that location.
170	
171	## Cross-linking (recompile)
172	
173	After journal entries or new entity creation, update related files:
174	
175	### After journal entry
176	For each person mentioned:
177	- Read their People file in wiki/People/
178	- Use `edit_file` to add updates to the managed section (above ## My Thoughts)
179	- Format for Timeline: `- YYYY-MM-DD brief description`
180	
181	### After new entity
182	- Search vault for existing files that mention the entity name
183	- Use `edit_file` to add [[wikilink]] to the new entity in those files (managed sections only)
184	- Only update files in wiki/ (respect the My Thoughts boundary)
185	
186	### Caps
187	- Max 5 files updated per recompile
188	- Grep before write (skip files that already contain the link)
189	- Only recompile for entities from the current session
190	
191	## Tools
192	
193	### Native tools (available to worker)
194	- `read_file(path)` — read any vault file
195	- `write_file(path, content)` — create new files (stubs, journal entries)
196	- `edit_file(path, old_str, new_str)` — safe targeted edit: finds exact old_str and replaces with new_str. Fails safely if not found or ambiguous. **Always use this instead of write_file for existing files.**
197	- `list_files(path)` — list directory contents
198	- `search_vault(query)` — search vault by filename/content
199	- `git_push_vault(message)` — commit and push vault changes
200	
201	## The flow in practice
202	
203	User: "Had lunch with Alfredo at Dishoom today"
204	
205	1. Chat model dispatches to worker via `do_work`
206	2. Worker reads Alfredo's people file with `read_file`
207	3. Worker uses `edit_file` to update Alfredo's Timeline (append, never overwrite)
208	4. Worker checks if Dishoom exists with `search_vault("Dishoom")`
209	5. If not found, creates stub with `write_file("wiki/Places/Venues/Dishoom.md", ...)`
210	6. Worker appends to journal with wikilinks: `[[Alfredo]]`, `[[Dishoom]]`
211	7. User gets confirmation: "Logged. Dishoom is a new one, created a note for it."
212	
```

Good question. The distinction should be:

- **Domain files** (`domains/X/`) = rules and instructions for one specific intent
- **References** = stable reference material that could be needed across multiple domains

Looking at the actual usage:

| File | Used by | Cross-domain? |
|------|---------|---------------|
| `architecture.md` | system domain, validation (debugging), general orientation | Yes — multiple domains need it |
| `telegram.md` | any domain that adds/modifies commands | Yes — cross-cutting concern |
| `second-brain.md` | knowledge domain only | Not really — it's purely knowledge domain stuff |

`second-brain.md` is 212 lines of vault-specific rules (ownership, entity creation, cross-linking) that only the knowledge worker ever reads. It's basically an extension of the knowledge domain contract — could just as easily live at `domains/knowledge/vault.md`.

The other two make sense as references — they're genuinely cross-domain.

So the cleanest split would be: move `second-brain.md` into the knowledge domain (where it's actually used), and keep `references/` for truly shared material like architecture and telegram docs. Want me to do that?

> AGENT

Interesting — `fitness_web` isn't independent. It imports from `src.fitness_db` (the shared database layer). So it needs to be a sibling of `src/` to resolve those imports.

Here's the reality of `argus/`:

| Folder | Belongs here? | Why |
|--------|--------------|-----|
| `src/` | Yes | Core agent code, reads ICM files from ARGUS_HOME |
| `domains/` | Yes | ICM context, read by `src/` |
| `references/` | Yes | ICM context, read by `src/` |
| `fitness_web/` | Yes | Imports `from src.fitness_db`, shares venv and .env |
| `services/` | Arguably not | Systemd unit files — deployment config, not application code |

`fitness_web` and `src` are coupled through the database module, so they need to stay siblings. Both systemd services run with `WorkingDirectory=/root/argus-agents/argus`, same venv, same `.env`.

The only thing that feels out of place is `services/` — those are deployment artifacts like `deploy.sh`, which already lives at the repo root. They could move up alongside it. But it's a minor thing and not really worth the churn of updating symlinks on the VPS.

Short answer: the structure actually makes sense for how things are wired. It's not an accident.

A few options:

1. **claude.ai/code** — Claude Code has a web app that works in a phone browser. Easiest path, no setup needed. Though I'm not sure about the specifics of connecting it to your VPS repo.

2. **Claude Code CLI on the VPS** — Install it directly on the VPS where the code lives. Access it via a mobile SSH client (Termius, Blink). The VPS is always on, sessions persist. Terminal on a phone isn't amazing UX, but it works.

3. **Web terminal on VPS** — Run Claude Code CLI on the VPS behind something like `ttyd` to give you a browser-based terminal at a URL. Combined with option 2, you'd get something like `term.argbox.net` that you open on your phone. A bit hacky but functional.

4. **Remote triggers** — Claude Code has scheduled agents that run on Anthropic's infrastructure against your repo. Good for automated tasks but not interactive sessions.

Honestly, for your setup, option 2 is probably the most practical — you already have the VPS, the code is there, and you're comfortable with SSH. You'd just need a decent mobile SSH client.

Perfect! Now I have comprehensive information to answer all of the user's questions. Let me provide a detailed response.

---

## Summary of Claude Code Mobile & Remote Access Options

Here's what's **actually available** vs. speculative:

### 1. **claude.ai/code — Is it a full web-based experience? Mobile access?**

**YES, it's real.** `claude.ai/code` is a full web interface for Claude Code running on **Anthropic-managed cloud infrastructure** (not your machine). Key facts:

- **Status**: Research preview (available for Pro, Max, Team, and Enterprise users)
- **Sessions persist** even if you close the browser
- **Mobile monitoring**: You can view sessions from the Claude mobile app (iOS & Android)
- **NOT a code editor on mobile**: You can't edit code directly from a phone browser—it's mainly for monitoring and steering sessions
- **Full GitHub integration**: Clones repos, creates PRs, auto-fix CI failures

**For your VPS workflow**: This won't help directly because claude.ai/code runs in Anthropic's cloud, not on your VPS.

---

### 2. **Can claude.ai/code connect to a remote repo/server (SSH)?**

**Not directly.** claude.ai/code:
- Works with **GitHub only** (no SSH to arbitrary VPS)
- Clones your repo from GitHub into the cloud sandbox
- Does NOT have SSH support to external servers
- Cannot execute against your Hetzner VPS in real-time

**However**, there's an indirect path: You can use `claude --remote` from your terminal to send tasks to the cloud, and the cloud session can clone your repo and push branches back to GitHub. But it won't run *on* your VPS.

---

### 3. **Remote Control — The actual game-changer for your phone**

**This is what you probably want.** Remote Control (February 2026, research preview):

- **What it does**: Runs Claude Code on *your local machine* but exposes it to your phone, tablet, or browser
- **Mobile access**: Full control from the Claude iOS/Android app or any browser via `claude.ai/code`
- **SSH/VPS compatible**: Your machine stays local, so all your MCP servers, tools, and **SSH connections** are available
- **Server mode**: Run `claude remote-control` and Claude keeps running on your machine while you steer from your phone
- **Works with your setup**: Since your repo is on your VPS and you SSH into it, Remote Control can interact with your VPS the same way your local terminal does

**Setup** (this is key):
```bash
# On your machine (laptop or server):
claude remote-control

# On your phone:
# Scan the QR code shown in terminal → opens in Claude app
# OR open the session URL in any browser
```

**The limitation**: Your machine (or the machine running `claude remote-control`) must stay on and connected to the internet. If your laptop sleeps, the session ends. If you want 24/7 availability, run it on your VPS itself.

---

### 4. **Self-hosted web GUI options**

Several open-source alternatives exist if you want a custom web interface without being tied to Anthropic's infrastructure:

**Free/Open-source options**:
- **[CloudCLI](https://github.com/siteboon/claudecodeui)** (GitHub repo) — free open-source web UI, self-hosted, manage Claude Code sessions remotely
- **[Claude Code Viewer](https://github.com/d-kimuson/claude-code-viewer)** — full-featured web client for Claude Code
- **[247 by QuivrHQ](https://github.com/QuivrHQ/247-claude-code-remote)** — mobile-first web terminal with Cloudflare Tunnel (no port forwarding needed), PWA installable on phone
- **[claude-web](https://github.com/matalvernaz/claude-web)** — self-hostable web UI with native OIDC auth

These let you run a web server on your VPS and access Claude Code from any browser (including your phone).

---

### 5. **Claude Code desktop app — remote capabilities?**

**The desktop app doesn't have native web/mobile features**, but:
- It supports Remote Control with the `/remote-control` command
- VS Code extension also supports Remote Control
- Desktop app can send sessions to the web with "Continue in" menu

---

## Your Best Options

**For phone access to your VPS-based workflow**, ranked:

| Option | Setup | Mobile? | Requires Local Machine? | VPS Support |
|--------|-------|---------|------------------------|-------------|
| **Remote Control** (Anthropic) | `claude remote-control` on laptop | ✓ Full | ✓ (must be on) | ✓ Via SSH from machine |
| **Remote Control on VPS** | `claude remote-control` run directly on VPS via SSH | ✓ Full | ✗ No | ✓ Direct |
| **CloudCLI self-hosted** | Self-host on VPS, open port/tunnel | ✓ Browser only | ✗ No | ✓ Direct |
| **claude.ai/code (cloud)** | Use `--remote` from terminal | ✓ Monitoring only | ✓ (to trigger) | ✗ No |

---

## Recommended Setup for You

Given you have a Hetzner VPS with your repo:

**Option A (Simplest, requires laptop on)**:
```bash
# From your laptop, SSH into the VPS:
ssh argus
cd /path/to/repo
claude remote-control --name "Argus Project"

# Scan QR code on phone → control from Claude app
```

**Option B (Always-on, best for VPS)**:
```bash
# SSH into your VPS and run Claude Code there:
ssh argus
claude remote-control --name "Argus VPS Sessions"

# Access from phone anytime via claude.ai/code or Claude app
# Your VPS stays on 24/7
```

Option B requires Claude Code CLI installed on your VPS, but then your phone can access it anytime without your laptop being on.

---

## Sources:

- [Claude Code on the web](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/claude-code-on-the-web.md)
- [Remote Control documentation](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/remote-control.md)
- [Claude Code Remote Control: Complete Setup Guide (2026)](https://claudefa.st/blog/guide/development/remote-control-guide)
- [VentureBeat: Anthropic releases Remote Control for Claude Code](https://venturebeat.com/orchestration/anthropic-just-released-a-mobile-version-of-claude-code-called-remote)
- [CloudCLI GitHub](https://github.com/siteboon/claudecodeui)
- [247 Claude Code Remote (QuivrHQ)](https://github.com/QuivrHQ/247-claude-code-remote)
- [Claude Code VPS Setup Guide](https://claudefa.st/blog/guide/development/infraops-vps-guide)

Based on the search results, here's what I found about Claude Code web GUI self-hosted VPS mobile access in 2026:

## Official Claude Code Remote Features

Claude Code has a Remote feature that allows you to securely bridge your VPS environment to the Claude mobile app or web interface. This ensures 24/7 availability, allowing you to monitor and manage long-running coding tasks from any device.

However, it's important to note that Claude Code's Remote Control feature (released Feb 2026) lets you control a session from your phone, but the process still runs on your local machine. A VPS setup is the only path to true close-the-laptop persistence.

## VPS Setup Solutions

For self-hosting on a VPS with mobile access, several options are available:

- **CloudCLI**: CloudCLI is a free open source webui/GUI that helps you manage Claude Code sessions and projects remotely on mobile and web.

- **Happy Server**: Running your own Happy Server takes 3 minutes and gives you permanent control over your Claude Code mobile access.

- **OpenClaw**: OpenClaw is an open-source gateway that bridges AI agents to messaging platforms and runs AI coding agents on any server, bridging them to WhatsApp, Telegram, Discord, iMessage, and more.

## Hosting Options

VPS providers confirmed to work include Hetzner, Vultr, DigitalOcean, Hostinger, and Fasthosts. A typical VPS setup costs $5–10/month plus Claude Pro at $20/month for a total of $25–30/month.

Based on the search results, here's what I found about OpenCode web GUI and how it works as a self-hosted terminal:

## OpenCode Overview

OpenCode is an open source AI coding agent designed to work in your terminal (TUI + CLI) with additional desktop/IDE surfaces. OpenCode is a Go-based CLI application that brings AI assistance to your terminal and provides a TUI (Terminal User Interface) for interacting with various AI models to help with coding tasks, debugging, and more.

## Web GUI Implementation

While OpenCode itself is primarily terminal-based, there are community projects that provide web GUI interfaces:

1. **opencode-web**: A web-based UI for interacting with the opencode API. OpenCode can run a headless HTTP server (opencode serve) with an OpenAPI spec at /doc, and it provides an official JS/TS SDK.

2. **OpenCode Manager**: A Node.js backend that adds universal LLM support, allowing you to bring your own key for OpenAI, Anthropic, Gemini, or a local model, with AuthService managing secure credential storage.

3. **OpenGUI**: A desktop + web command center for coding agents that runs OpenCode, Claude Code, Codex, and Pi across multiple projects with streaming chat, prompt queue, model switching, voice input, and MCP tools.

## Self-Hosted Support

You can use a self-hosted model by setting the LOCAL_ENDPOINT environment variable, which will cause OpenCode to load and use the models from the specified endpoint. OpenCode supports self-hosted models through a local endpoint configuration, and if you are running a compatible model server on your own infrastructure, you can point OpenCode to it and use it the same way you would use a cloud provider.
