> DEVELOPER

Okay, so the thing is that in the future I would want my girlfriend to be able to work on this repo of like the August repo to extend it and add functionality, but I want things to be separate in the sense of like my obsidian vault which is private to me, etcetera etcetera. So how could I have it so that she can still edit and work on the project? For example, right now in terms of the telegram, it's private in the sense of her telegram vault can only do certain things, mine can only do certain things. And I was thinking of having it so that she has to open up pull requests. But how can I prevent like her changing the code to read my obsidian vault by accident or like this things like I'm not really sure what I can do to just to solve this

> AGENT

Good question. The key insight is there are two layers of protection:

**Layer 1: She never has VPS access.** Even if code changes got merged, she can't deploy them. The vault, Strava tokens, and private data all live on your VPS. No SSH key = no access.

**Layer 2: PR reviews before anything merges.** She works on branches, opens PRs, you review before merging. Combined with a CODEOWNERS file, GitHub won't let PRs merge without your approval on sensitive files.

Here's what I'd set up:

1. **Branch protection on `master`** — no direct pushes, PRs required, your review required
2. **CODEOWNERS file** — marks sensitive files (agent.py, tools.py, config.py, domains/) as requiring your approval
3. **She gets GitHub collaborator access** — can push branches and open PRs, but can't merge to master without your approval
4. **She never gets VPS SSH access** — you're the only one who deploys
5. **Separate dev .env** — she'd have a dev setup with mock/test values, no real tokens

The honest truth: the strongest protection is that the code is useless without your VPS and secrets. She could write code that reads the vault, but it would only work on your machine where you control deployment. The PR review is really just a safety net for accidental changes.

Want me to set up the branch protection and CODEOWNERS?

> DEVELOPER

Okay so the way it works is that neither of us every work directly on the VPS then and can't chnage code there? she has to do it locally?

> AGENT

Exactly. The VPS only ever does `git pull` from GitHub — nobody edits code there. Both of you work locally, push branches, open PRs. You review and merge. Then you deploy with `ssh argus "cd /root/argus-agents && git pull"`.

So the flow is:

```
Ashlyn (local) → branch → PR → Your review → merge to master
You (local)    → branch → PR → merge to master (you're the owner)
                                      ↓
                         ssh argus "git pull" → live
```

She never touches the VPS. You control what gets deployed and when.

Want me to set up branch protection + CODEOWNERS now?

> DEVELOPER

but like can she clone it locally and check if it works? becuase like the vikunka stuff ect she should only have access to cerain projoects and the vikunja lives in the vps

> AGENT

Good point. She doesn't need VPS SSH access to test — the services are already exposed via HTTPS:

- `task.argbox.net` — Vikunja (she already has a user account, id=2, limited to shared projects)
- `llm.argbox.net` — LiteLLM
- `notes.argbox.net` — Flatnotes

So she'd clone the repo, create her own `argus/.env` that points at the public URLs instead of localhost:

```
# Her .env (connects to VPS services over HTTPS)
VIKUNJA_API_URL=https://task.argbox.net/api/v1
LITELLM_BASE_URL=https://llm.argbox.net/v1
FLATNOTES_URL=https://notes.argbox.net

# Her own test bot
TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN=<her-test-bot-token>
TELEGRAM_USERS=67890:member:Ashlyn

# No vault, no Strava — she doesn't need them
VAULT_PATH=
```

She can run the agent locally, test against real services, but the role system restricts her to member-level access. No vault, no Strava, no journal. Just shared tasks and notes — same as production.

She never needs SSH. The APIs are already public with auth. Want me to create a `dev.env.example` for her setup and set up the branch protection + CODEOWNERS?

> DEVELOPER

Okay bit what if she wants to exctrend the task management capabilites. She can't test this locally becasue all the tickets are on the VPS rght!

> AGENT

No — she CAN test it locally because the Vikunja API is public at `task.argbox.net`. Her local code would call that URL over HTTPS, hitting the same Vikunja instance on the VPS. She doesn't need SSH for that.

It's the same as how you use any cloud API — the code runs on your laptop, the service runs on a server. She'd just need a Vikunja API token scoped to her user (id=2), which only sees Project Pyari (the shared project).

```
Her laptop                          Your VPS
─────────                          ────────
argus code (local)  ──HTTPS──→  task.argbox.net (Vikunja)
                                    └─ her token only sees shared projects
                    ──HTTPS──→  llm.argbox.net (LiteLLM)
                    ──HTTPS──→  notes.argbox.net (Flatnotes)
```

She'd see real tickets, create real tasks, test real changes — all through the public API. Just can't see your personal/inbox/research projects because her Vikunja user doesn't have access to those.

Want me to set everything up — her dev env example, branch protection, and CODEOWNERS?

> DEVELOPER

But she could edit the code to access my vikunja right. Or for example edit the code to acces my obsidan vault

> AGENT

Right, she could write code that tries to. But here's why it doesn't matter:

**Her local machine has no access to your vault.** The vault lives at `/root/codex` on the VPS. Her laptop doesn't have that path. Even if she wrote `read_file("/root/codex/personal/me/Sumeet.md")`, it would just fail — the file doesn't exist on her machine.

**Her Vikunja token only sees shared projects.** Even if she changed the code to call `list_all_tasks`, the API would only return tasks from projects her user has access to. That's enforced by Vikunja itself, not by your code.

**The only risk is if the code gets deployed to the VPS.** On the VPS, `/root/codex` exists and the admin Vikunja token sees everything. But that's where PR reviews come in — you review her changes before merging, and you control deployment.

So the layers are:

| Attack | Why it fails |
|--------|-------------|
| Read vault locally | Path doesn't exist on her machine |
| Read your Vikunja projects locally | Her API token is scoped to shared projects only |
| Merge code that reads vault | PR requires your review — you'd catch it |
| Deploy malicious code to VPS | She has no SSH access |

The only scenario where it breaks is if you approve a PR without reading it AND deploy it. The CODEOWNERS file makes that harder by flagging changes to sensitive files like `tools.py` and `agent.py`.

The vault.md question is really: does she need to see the vault structure to contribute to Argus?

The vault.md is loaded by the **worker agent** when handling knowledge tasks (journal entries, entity creation, cross-linking). If she's extending task management or notes features, she doesn't need it at all.

But if she ever works on the knowledge domain, she'd need to understand how the vault works — just not the personal folder structure.

Options:

1. **Split vault.md** — Keep the vault rules (ownership, entity creation, cross-linking) in git, but move the folder tree that shows `personal/relationship/proposal` etc. to a file on the VPS only (not in git). The worker loads it at runtime.

2. **Redact the personal section** — Replace the detailed personal folder tree with just `personal/ — user-owned, Argus reads only` without listing the subfolders. The worker doesn't need to know about `relationship/` or `remembering_nanaji/` to function correctly — it just needs to know the folder exists and is read-only.

3. **Move vault.md entirely out of git** — Put it on the VPS only, loaded at runtime. But then she can't work on knowledge features at all.

I'd go with option 2 — the worker only needs to know `personal/` is read-only. It doesn't need the subfolder listing to do its job. You keep the file in git, she can work on knowledge features, but your private folder names stay private.

1	# Second Brain System
2	
3	## What this is
4	
5	Argus actively manages the Obsidian vault at /root/codex/ as a personal knowledge graph. It looks up people, logs daily events, creates entity notes, and maintains cross-links between everything.
6	
7	## Core principle: My Thoughts boundary
8	
9	Every file in `wiki/` has a `## My Thoughts` section. Argus manages everything ABOVE this line (facts, links, interaction history). Everything BELOW is the user's personal space. Argus can rewrite its managed section when facts change, but never touches the user's section.
10	
11	## AI-created notes
12	
13	All notes created by Argus have frontmatter:
14	```yaml
15	---
16	created_by: argus
17	created: YYYY-MM-DD
18	type: person|place|media|concept|organisation
19	---
20	```
21	
22	Search `created_by: argus` to find all AI-created notes.
23	
24	## Vault structure
25	
26	```
27	/root/codex/
28	  wiki/                          <- Argus-managed (## My Thoughts boundary)
29	    People/                      <- people files with YAML frontmatter + aliases
30	    Places/
31	      Countries/                 <- countries and cities (India, France, Berlin, etc)
32	      Cities/                    <- standalone city notes
33	      Venues/                    <- restaurants, museums, etc (created as needed)
34	      Completed/                 <- completed trip notes
35	    Media/
36	      Books/                     <- book notes
37	      Movies/                    <- created as needed
38	      Music/                     <- music notes
39	      Comics/                    <- reading list
40	      Art/                       <- Pre-Raphaelites, Renaissance, Academic, Misc
41	    Knowledge/
42	      Religion/                  <- Sikhism, Hinduism, Christianity, Buddhism, Greek Mythology, etc
43	      History/                   <- Romans, French Revolution, Pre-History, etc
44	      Science/                   <- created as needed
45	      Computing/                 <- created as needed
46	    Organisations/               <- created as needed
47	
48	  journal/                       <- daily entries (Argus appends events, user writes reflections)
49	
50	  personal/                      <- user-owned, Argus reads only
51	    me/                          <- Sumeet.md, compass.md, the-log.md, rituals, block-tasks
52	    health/                      <- fitness routines, quitting vaping
53	    relationship/                <- proposal, life partner questions
54	    remembering_nanaji/          <- memorial (music, speech, bereavement)
55	    notes/                       <- misc personal notes
56	
57	  projects/                      <- user-owned
58	    reliq/                       <- freelance work (contracts, invoices, notes)
59	    matrix/                      <- technical learning (agents, languages, home env)
60	
61	  creative/                      <- user-owned
62	    poetry/
63	    ideas/
64	    scratch/
65	    workshop/
66	
67	  finance/                       <- financial tracking
68	  Images/                        <- Obsidian paste images
69	  Docs/                          <- PDFs
70	```
71	
72	## Ownership rules
73	
74	| Folder | Who manages | Argus can |
75	|--------|-------------|-----------|
76	| wiki/ | Argus (above ## My Thoughts) | Create, read, update, cross-link |
77	| journal/ | Shared (Argus appends events, user writes reflections) | Append only |
78	| personal/ | User only | Read only |
79	| projects/ | User only | Read only |
80	| creative/ | User only | Read only |
81	| finance/ | User only | Read only |
82	
83	## People lookup
84	
85	People files live in `wiki/People/`. Files have YAML frontmatter with aliases.
86	
87	```yaml
88	---
89	aliases: [fufarji, fufar]
90	tags: [person/family]
91	---
92	```
93	
94	Lookup order:
95	1. Exact filename match
96	2. Alias match from frontmatter
97	3. Fuzzy match (difflib, cutoff 0.6)
98	4. Vault-wide grep fallback
99	
100	When looking up a person, also grep `journal/` for recent mentions to answer "when did I last see them?"
101	
102	## Daily journal logging
103	
104	When the user mentions events, Argus appends to today's journal.
105	
106	File path: `journal/YYYY-MM-DD.md`
107	Format:
108	```markdown
109	# YYYY-MM-DD
110	
111	**09:30** Had coffee with [[Alfredo]] at [[Monmouth Coffee]]
112	
113	**14:00** Visited [[British Museum]], saw the Rosetta Stone exhibition
114	
115	## My Thoughts
116	(user writes here)
117	```
118	
119	If the file doesn't exist, create it. If it does, append.
120	Wikilink all known people and entities with [[Name]] syntax.
121	
122	## Entity creation
123	
124	When a new entity is mentioned and discussed (not just a passing reference), create a stub.
125	
126	### Creation thresholds
127	- People: always create in `wiki/People/`
128	- Places: create if visited, planned, or discussed in detail
129	- Media (movies, shows, podcasts): create if opinioned about or discussed
130	- Books: create in `wiki/Media/Books/`
131	- Concepts: create only if a dedicated discussion happens
132	- Events: do NOT create, events live in the journal
133	
134	### Stub template
135	```markdown
136	---
137	created_by: argus
138	created: YYYY-MM-DD
139	type: place
140	---
141	# Dishoom
142	
143	Indian restaurant in [[London]].
144	
145	First mentioned: YYYY-MM-DD
146	
147	Related: [[Alfredo]], [[Indian Cuisine]]
148	Mentioned in: [[journal/YYYY-MM-DD]]
149	
150	## My Thoughts
151	
152	```
153	
154	### Intelligent linking (Level 2)
155	When creating a stub, Argus uses its general knowledge to infer related entities. Dishoom is an Indian restaurant, so link to [[India]] and [[London]] if those notes exist. Only link to entities that already exist in the vault. Don't create chain stubs.
156	
157	### File placement
158	| Type | Location |
159	|------|----------|
160	| Person | wiki/People/{Name}.md |
161	| Place (country/city) | wiki/Places/Countries/{Name}.md |
162	| Place (venue) | wiki/Places/Venues/{Name}.md |
163	| Book | wiki/Media/Books/{Name}.md |
164	| Movie/Show | wiki/Media/Movies/{Name}.md |
165	| Art | wiki/Media/Art/{Name}.md |
166	| Concept | wiki/Knowledge/{best-fit subfolder}/{Name}.md |
167	| Organisation | wiki/Organisations/{Name}.md |
168	
169	Always search the vault first. If an entity already exists anywhere, use that location.
170	
171	## Cross-linking (recompile)
172	
173	After journal entries or new entity creation, update related files:
174	
175	### After journal entry
176	For each person mentioned:
177	- Read their People file in wiki/People/
178	- Use `edit_file` to add updates to the managed section (above ## My Thoughts)
179	- Format for Timeline: `- YYYY-MM-DD brief description`
180	
181	### After new entity
182	- Search vault for existing files that mention the entity name
183	- Use `edit_file` to add [[wikilink]] to the new entity in those files (managed sections only)
184	- Only update files in wiki/ (respect the My Thoughts boundary)
185	
186	### Caps
187	- Max 5 files updated per recompile
188	- Grep before write (skip files that already contain the link)
189	- Only recompile for entities from the current session
190	
191	## Tools
192	
193	### Native tools (available to worker)
194	- `read_file(path)` — read any vault file
195	- `write_file(path, content)` — create new files (stubs, journal entries)
196	- `edit_file(path, old_str, new_str)` — safe targeted edit: finds exact old_str and replaces with new_str. Fails safely if not found or ambiguous. **Always use this instead of write_file for existing files.**
197	- `list_files(path)` — list directory contents
198	- `search_vault(query)` — search vault by filename/content
199	- `git_push_vault(message)` — commit and push vault changes
200	
201	## The flow in practice
202	
203	User: "Had lunch with Alfredo at Dishoom today"
204	
205	1. Chat model dispatches to worker via `do_work`
206	2. Worker reads Alfredo's people file with `read_file`
207	3. Worker uses `edit_file` to update Alfredo's Timeline (append, never overwrite)
208	4. Worker checks if Dishoom exists with `search_vault("Dishoom")`
209	5. If not found, creates stub with `write_file("wiki/Places/Venues/Dishoom.md", ...)`
210	6. Worker appends to journal with wikilinks: `[[Alfredo]]`, `[[Dishoom]]`
211	7. User gets confirmation: "Logged. Dishoom is a new one, created a note for it."
212	
```

## Approved Plan:
# Move Vault Structure Out of Git Into the Vault Itself

## Context
The file `argus/domains/knowledge/vault.md` contains both operational rules (how Argus manages the vault) and the private vault folder structure (showing personal subfolders like `relationship/proposal`, `health/quitting vaping`). When Ashlyn gets repo access, she'd see this. The rules are fine to share — the folder map is not.

## Changes

### 1. Create `/root/codex/VAULT.md` on VPS
Move the vault structure section (the folder tree from lines 24-70) into a file inside the vault itself. The worker can read it at runtime with `read_file("VAULT.md")`.

### 2. Strip the folder tree from `argus/domains/knowledge/vault.md`
Remove the `## Vault structure` section (the detailed folder tree). Replace it with a one-liner telling the worker to read the vault's own docs:

```markdown
## Vault structure
Read `VAULT.md` at the vault root for the full folder structure. Run `read_file("VAULT.md")` before creating or placing files.
```

Keep everything else — rules, ownership table, entity creation, cross-linking, tools, examples. The ownership table (lines 72-81) just lists top-level folders (`personal/`, `projects/`, etc.) without revealing subfolders — that's fine.

### 3. Update knowledge domain contract
`argus/domains/knowledge/CONTEXT.md` already says "See domains/knowledge/vault.md for full vault structure." No change needed — the vault.md still exists in git, it just defers to the vault's own VAULT.md for the folder tree.

## Files
- `argus/domains/knowledge/vault.md` — remove folder tree, add pointer to vault VAULT.md
- `/root/codex/VAULT.md` (on VPS via SSH) — new file with the folder structure

## Verification
- Check vault.md in git has no private folder names
- `ssh argus "cat /root/codex/VAULT.md"` shows the full structure
- Worker can still access it via `read_file("VAULT.md")`

```

2	
3	Two-tier Telegram bot on a Hetzner VPS. A fast chat model talks to users, a smart worker model executes tasks with tools and domain context.
4	
5	## Repo Structure
6	
7	```
8	argus/          — Agent code + ICM context (Python 3.12)
9	infra/          — Docker services (litellm, flatnotes, vikunja, nanoproxy)
10	services/       — Systemd unit files
11	nginx/          — Reverse proxy configs (deployed via stow)
12	deploy.sh       — Deploy nginx + systemd to VPS
13	```
14	
15	## Architecture
16	
17	```
18	Telegram → Chat model (argus-chat) → simple reply
19	                                    → do_work(task, intent) → Worker model (argus-worker)
20	                                                               ↓
21	                                                          ICM context + filtered tools
22	                                                               ↓
23	                                                          Result → Chat model → User
24	```
25	
26	- **Chat model**: Handles conversation, classifies intent, dispatches complex tasks to worker
27	- **Worker model**: Full ICM context, all tools, narrates progress via status callbacks
28	- **ICM layers**: `argus/CLAUDE.md` (spec + routing) → `domains/*/CONTEXT.md` (contracts) → `references/*.md` (stable docs)
29	- **Tool filtering**: Worker gets only tools matching the intent (tasks, fitness, knowledge, etc.)
30	- **Role gating**: Admin (Sumeet) = full access, Member (Ashlyn) = shared tasks + notes only
31	
32	## Key Files
33	
34	| File | Purpose |
35	|------|---------|
36	| `argus/src/agent.py` | Two-tier orchestration, tool routing, LLM calls |
37	| `argus/src/context.py` | Builds system prompts for chat + worker models |
38	| `argus/src/config.py` | Env vars, user roles, model names |
39	| `argus/src/tools.py` | Native tool definitions (vault, fitness, notes, files) |
40	| `argus/src/mcp_client.py` | MCP server manager (Vikunja task tools) |
41	| `argus/src/telegram_bot.py` | Telegram interface, command handlers |
42	| `argus/src/fitness_db.py` | Fitness SQLite (workouts, measurements, Strava) |
43	| `argus/src/db.py` | Conversation history SQLite |
44	| `argus/fitness_web/app.py` | Flask dashboard (fit.argbox.net) |
45	| `argus/CLAUDE.md` | Worker agent spec + domain routing table |
46	
47	## Development
48	
49	### Setup (local)
50	```bash
51	cd argus && python3 -m venv venv && source venv/bin/activate
52	pip install -r requirements.txt
53	cp .env.example .env  # fill in tokens
54	```
55	
56	### Deploy to VPS
57	```bash
58	ssh argus "cd /root/argus-agents && git pull"
59	ssh argus "bash /root/argus-agents/deploy.sh"        # nginx + systemd
60	ssh argus "systemctl restart argus"                    # restart agent
61	```
62	
63	### VPS access
64	```bash
65	ssh argus                                # Hetzner VPS
66	journalctl -u argus -f                   # agent logs
67	journalctl -u fitness_web -f             # dashboard logs
68	docker logs -f flatnotes                 # notes app logs
69	```
70	
71	### Docker services (on VPS)
72	```bash
73	cd /root/argus-agents/infra/litellm && docker compose up -d
74	cd /root/argus-agents/infra/flatnotes && docker compose up -d
75	cd /root/argus-agents/infra/vikunja && docker compose up -d
76	cd /root/argus-agents/infra/nanoproxy && docker compose up -d
77	```
78	
79	## Services
80	
81	| Service | Type | Port | URL |
82	|---------|------|------|-----|
83	| Argus | systemd | — | Telegram: @argus_custom_ai_bot |
84	| Fitness Dashboard | systemd | 5050 | https://fit.argbox.net |
85	| OpenCode | systemd | 4096 | https://code.argbox.net |
86	| LiteLLM | Docker | 4000 | https://llm.argbox.net |
87	| Vikunja | Docker | 3456 | https://task.argbox.net |
88	| Flatnotes | Docker | 8089 | https://notes.argbox.net |
89	| NanoProxy | Docker | 8787 | internal only |
90	
91	## Conventions
92	
93	- **No tests yet** — verify changes via Telegram or fitness dashboard manually
94	- **Secrets in .env files** — never commit secrets; `.env` is gitignored
95	- **Domain contracts are markdown** — `domains/*/CONTEXT.md` defines rules per intent
96	- **Chat prompts are per-user** — `domains/chat/sumeet.md` (admin), `domains/chat/ashlyn.md` (member)
97	- **fitness_web imports from src/** — they share `src/fitness_db.py`, must stay siblings
98	- **Nginx via stow** — configs in `nginx/etc/nginx/sites-enabled/`, deployed with `deploy.sh`
99	- **Flatnotes data backed up** — cron pushes `infra/flatnotes/data/` to a separate GitHub repo every 15 min
100	
101	## Gotchas
102	
103	- `argus/src/mcp_client.py` runs MCP in a daemon thread with its own async event loop — don't mix sync/async carelessly
104	- The chat model can call simple tools directly (create_task, list_tasks) — `do_work` is only for multi-step operations
105	- `INTENT_TOOLS` in `agent.py` controls which tools the worker gets per intent — add new tools here
106	- Vikunja port in docker-compose is 3456 but some nginx configs may reference 3000 — use 3456
107	- Python deps aren't installed locally (runs on VPS) — `import` checks will fail on your laptop
108	- The worker's `argus/CLAUDE.md` is NOT this file — it's the ICM Layer 0 prompt for the AI worker agent
109	
```

1	---
2	name: Argus project context
3	description: Architecture and design decisions for the Argus personal AI agent system
4	type: project
5	---
6	
7	Building "Argus" — a personal AI agent with Telegram as primary interface.
8	
9	**Architecture (current):**
10	- Two-tier agent: fast chat model (deepseek-chat) + smart worker model (glm-4.6), both via NanoGPT subscription through LiteLLM
11	- Multi-module Python codebase at `argus/src/` (agent.py, tools.py, telegram_bot.py, context.py, journal_bot.py, mcp_client.py, db.py, config.py, strava.py, fitness_db.py)
12	- NanoProxy bridges unreliable tool calling for open-source models
13	- Van Clief ICM: CLAUDE.md (Layer 0) -> CONTEXT.md (Layer 1) -> domain contracts (Layer 2) -> references (Layer 3)
14	- Domains: chat, tasks, system, validation, knowledge (fitness domain planned)
15	- Vikunja for task management via MCP (23 tools including create_project, assign_user)
16	- Obsidian vault at /root/codex/ as knowledge graph
17	- Strava integration: direct API client with auto-refreshing OAuth tokens
18	- Fitness DB: SQLite with activities, splits, laps, best_efforts, segments, streams, exercises, sets, measurements
19	- Fitness dashboard: fit.argbox.net (Flask + Mapbox + Chart.js + Savitzky-Golay)
20	- Separate journal bot (pure logging, no AI) on its own Telegram bot
21	- SQLite for conversation history (no MemPalace — removed)
22	- HTML parse_mode on Telegram messages with auto-formatting URLs
23	- Worker collects URLs from tool results and appends missing ones to output
24	- Roundup: silent mode, edit_file only, Savitzky-Golay for pace charts
25	
26	**Vikunja projects:** Inbox (1), Argus (2), Personal (3), Research (4), Project Pyari (5, shared with Ashlyn)
27	**Vikunja users:** Sumeet (id=1), Ashlyn (id=2, Project Pyari only)
28	
29	**Design philosophy:**
30	- Van Clief's ICM: plain text as universal interface, folder structure as architecture
31	- 60/30/10 rule: 60% traditional code, 30% rule-based, 10% AI
32	- Glass-box: everything is readable files, no black-box frameworks
33

Based on the search results, here's a comprehensive guide for getting started with OpenCode with free models:

## Installation

The recommended installation method is using curl: `curl -fsSL https://opencode.ai/install | bash`. Alternative methods include:
- Homebrew: `brew install anomalyco/tap/opencode`
- npm: `npm i -g opencode-ai@latest`
- Windows Scoop: `scoop install opencode`

## Getting Started with Free Models

OpenCode includes free models included or you can connect any model from any provider, including Claude, GPT, Gemini and more.

**Basic Setup Steps:**

1. Start OpenCode with `opencode`
2. In the TUI, run `/connect` and select "opencode"
3. Visit https://opencode.ai/auth, sign up and add billing, then copy your API key

**Using Models:**

- Run `/models` in the TUI to see the list of recommended models
- Start with free models to learn, then upgrade as needed - most developers spend $5-20/month

## Free Model Options

There are tools designed to help you utilize free models without spending on API keys or subscriptions by leveraging OpenCode Zen free tier and Google Gemini free tier limits, providing a generous allowance for daily, medium-complexity tasks at no cost.

## Key Features

OpenCode uses the AI SDK and Models.dev to support 75+ LLM providers and it supports running local models. OpenCode is an open source agent that helps you write code in your terminal, IDE, or desktop.

## Free Models and Lists

OpenCode includes free models or you can connect any model from any provider, including Claude, GPT, Gemini and more. The model list is a curated list of models that have been tested and verified by the OpenCode team.

## OpenCode Auth Setup

For authentication and login setup:

- Run the /connect command in the TUI, select opencode, and head to opencode.ai/auth to sign in, add your billing details, and copy your API key.

- To check if credentials are configured, run `opencode auth list` to see if credentials for the provider are added to your config.

## Beginner-Friendly Options

OpenCode supports 75+ Providers, including official subscription services like Claude Pro/Max and ChatGPT Plus/Pro, with methods that require no complex configuration suitable for quick experience.

For completely free and privacy-protected options, Ollama is a good choice - visit ollama.ai to download and install, then download models like Llama 3, DeepSeek Coder, or Qwen Coder.

OpenCode Go is a low cost subscription at $5 for your first month, then $10/month, providing generous limits and reliable access to the most capable open source models.

# Free Models in OpenCode

The provided documentation doesn't specify which models are completely free. However, the page indicates that "Most popular providers are preloaded by default" and mentions that OpenCode "supports **75+ LLM providers** and running local models."

## How to Select a Model

To choose a model, users can type the `/models` command within OpenCode, which displays available options based on configured providers.

## Default Model Setup

To set a default model in your configuration:

```json
{
  "model": "provider_id/model_id"
}
```

The documentation notes that "OpenCode starts up, it checks for models in the following priority order: 1) Command line flag, 2) Config file setting, 3) Last used model, 4) First model using internal priority."

**Note:** The documentation doesn't explicitly list which models have free tiers. You would need to check individual provider pages (like OpenAI, Anthropic, or Google) to determine free options. The site recommends trying models like Claude Opus, Claude Sonnet, and Gemini, but pricing details aren't included in this documentation.

# OpenCode Installation and Setup Guide

## Installation Commands

The primary installation method is:
```bash
curl -fsSL https://opencode.ai/install | bash
```

Alternative package managers include npm (`npm install -g opencode-ai`), Homebrew (`brew install anomalyco/tap/opencode`), and several others for Windows users.

## Getting Started Steps

After installation, navigate to your project and run `opencode`. The documentation recommends initializing the project with `/init`, which "will get OpenCode to analyze your project and create an `AGENTS.md` file in the project root."

Configuration involves running `/connect`, selecting a provider, and adding API credentials through their authentication portal.

## Free Models Availability

The documentation does not specify free models available out of the box. Instead, it states: "If you are new to using LLM providers, we recommend using [OpenCode Zen](/docs/zen). It's a curated list of models that have been tested and verified by the OpenCode team."

The setup process requires configuring "API keys for the LLM providers you want to use," suggesting that most models require paid accounts rather than free tier access.

# OpenCode Go Overview

**What is OpenCode Go?**

OpenCode Go is a subscription service designed to democratize access to advanced coding models. It's described as bringing "agentic coding to programmers around the world" with "generous limits and reliable access to the most capable open-source models."

**Pricing Structure**

The service costs "$5 for your first month, then $10/month" with the ability to cancel anytime. Users can also top up additional credit as needed.

**Included Models**

Go provides access to a comprehensive roster of open-source models, including:
- GLM-5.1 and GLM-5
- Kimi K2.6 and K2.5
- MiMo-V2.5-Pro and MiMo-V2.5
- Qwen3.6 Plus and Qwen3.5 Plus
- MiniMax M2.7 and M2.5
- DeepSeek V4 Pro and V4 Flash

**Free Tier**

The page mentions "free models" are available but doesn't detail specific limitations. The comparative pricing chart suggests a free tier exists with more restricted access compared to the Go subscription tiers.

**Key Feature**

Go works with "any agent," not just OpenCode, making it a flexible option for developers using various coding tools.

It can help you write code, fix bugs, create projects, and learn programming. It comes with free models so you can start without paying anything.\n\n## Install OpenCode\n\nOpen Terminal (on Mac) or your terminal app and run:\n\n```bash\ncurl -fsSL https://opencode.ai/install | bash\n```\n\nClose and reopen your terminal after installing.\n\nCheck it worked:\n\n```bash\nopencode --version\n```\n\n## Create Your First Project\n\nMake a folder for your project and open OpenCode in it:\n\n```bash\nmkdir my-first-app\ncd my-first-app\nopencode\n```\n\nThis opens the OpenCode interface in your terminal.\n\n## Set Up Free Models\n\nOpenCode comes with free models you can use straight away. Type `/models` to see available models\n\n**Option 2: Google Gemini (free tier)**\n\n1. Go to https://aistudio.google.com/apikey\n2. Create a free API key\n3. In OpenCode, type `/connect` and select \"Google\"\n4. Paste your API key\n\n## Using OpenCode\n\nOnce set up, just type what you want in plain English:\n\n- \"Create a simple website with a homepage and about page\"\n- \"Make a Python script that converts CSV to JSON\"\n- \"Build a to-do list app with HTML, CSS and JavaScript\"\n- \"Fix the bug in app.py\"\n\nOpenCode will write the code, create the files, and explain what it did.\n\n## Useful Commands\n\nType these in OpenCode (they start with `/`):\n\n| Command | What it does |\n|---------|-------------|\n| `/models` | See and switch between available models |\n| `/connect` | Add a new AI provider |\n| `/init` | Analyse your project and create a config file |\n| `/help` | See all available commands |\n| `/clear` | Clear the conversation |\n\n## Tips\n\n- **Start small** — ask it to create simple things first, then build up\n- **Be specific** — \"Create a Python Flask app with a /hello endpoint that returns JSON\" works better than \"make an app\"\n- **Ask it to explain** — you can say \"explain what this code does\" at any time\n- **It can read your files** — if you already have code, just open OpenCode in that folder and ask questions about it\n\n## Example: Create a Simple Website\n\n```\nYou: Create a simple personal website with:\n- A homepage with my name and a short bio\n- A nice colour scheme\n- A navigation bar\n- Mobile responsive\n\nUse HTML and CSS only, no frameworks.\n```\n\nOpenCode will create the HTML and CSS files. Perfect! Now I have all the information I need. Let me compile a comprehensive report:

---

## COMPREHENSIVE FITNESS WORKOUT UI ANALYSIS

### 1. CURRENT WORKOUT UI ARCHITECTURE

**Technology Stack:**
- Backend: Flask (Python)
- Frontend: Vanilla JavaScript (no frameworks)
- Templating: Jinja2
- Styling: Plain CSS (no preprocessors)
- Database: SQLite
- No external charting libraries currently used in workout view

**Page Route:** `/activity/w/<int:workout_id>` (Flask route at `/Users/sumeet/matrix/web/argus/argus/fitness_web/app.py:300-356`)

---

### 2. DATA STRUCTURES & DATABASE SCHEMA

**Workout Exercises Table** (`workout_exercises`):
Located in `/Users/sumeet/matrix/web/argus/argus/src/fitness_db.py:144-159`

```
CREATE TABLE workout_exercises (
    id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,
    workout_id INTEGER NOT NULL,
    exercise_id INTEGER NOT NULL,
    position INTEGER NOT NULL,           # Display order
    variation TEXT DEFAULT 'standard',   # negative, assisted, weighted, etc.
    phase TEXT DEFAULT 'main',           # warm-up, skills, main, cool-down
    target_sets INTEGER,
    target_reps INTEGER,
    target_duration_seconds INTEGER,     # For holds (plank, dead hang)
    target_rpe REAL,                     # Rate of Perceived Exertion
    target_weight REAL,                  # kg
    superset_group INTEGER,              # Same number = paired exercises
    notes TEXT,                          # Coaching cues
    rest_seconds INTEGER                 # REST TIME PER EXERCISE (field exists!)
);
```

**Key Field: `rest_seconds` is ALREADY in the database schema** (line 158 in fitness_db.py). It's used when creating workouts (line 569 in fitness_db.py) and passed from the AI workout generator.

**Sets Table** (`sets`):
```
CREATE TABLE sets (
    id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,
    workout_exercise_id INTEGER,
    set_number INTEGER,
    reps INTEGER,
    duration_seconds INTEGER,
    weight REAL,
    rpe REAL,
    notes TEXT,
    logged_at TEXT DEFAULT (datetime('now'))
);
```

---

### 3. CURRENT WORKOUT DETAIL PAGE DISPLAY

**Phase Grouping** (app.py:328-352):
- Exercises are grouped by phase in this order: warm-up → skills → main → cool-down
- Within each phase, exercises are further grouped into supersets (same `superset_group` value)
- Supersets are visually marked with a border-left and "Superset" label

**What Users Currently See:**
- Exercise name (display_name or name)
- Variation badge (if not 'standard')
- Target format: e.g., "3 x 8 reps @ 6.5kg RPE 7"
- Exercise notes (coaching cues from database)
- Set logging table with columns:
  - Set number
  - Reps/Duration input
  - Weight input (optional)
  - RPE input (1-10 scale)
- Add/remove set buttons
- Notes textarea
- Buttons to save progress or finish workout

**Auto-save behavior:**
- Saves to database every 800ms after user input (vanilla JS, `scheduleSave` function)
- Collects all set data, updates workout status to 'in_progress'
- Persists to sets table via `/activity/w/<int:workout_id>/log` (POST)

---

### 4. REST SECONDS STATUS

**Status: FIELD EXISTS BUT IS NEVER DISPLAYED**

Evidence:
- Database schema includes `rest_seconds` INTEGER in workout_exercises (fitness_db.py:158)
- Used in workout creation: `ex.get("rest_seconds")` (fitness_db.py:569)
- Passed from fitness domain context (CONTEXT.md:69: "Include rest_seconds on each exercise")
- **BUT**: Not fetched by the Flask query (app.py:308-315) when loading workout detail
- **NOT**: Displayed in the template (workout.html) — no `{{ ex.rest_seconds }}`
- **NO**: JavaScript rest timer UI exists

**Current Query** (app.py:308-315):
```python
exercises = conn.execute("""
    SELECT we.*, e.name as exercise_name, e.display_name as exercise_display_name,
           e.type as exercise_type, e.description as exercise_description
    FROM workout_exercises we
    JOIN exercises e ON we.exercise_id = e.id
    WHERE we.workout_id = ?
    ORDER BY we.position
""", (workout_id,)).fetchall()
```

This SELECT * includes rest_seconds, but it's just not used in the template.

---

### 5. EXERCISE GROUPING & PHASES

**How Exercises Are Grouped** (app.py:328-352):

1. **Phase grouping** (5 sections):
   - warm-up
   - skills
   - main
   - cool-down

2. **Superset grouping within each phase**:
   - If `superset_group` is NOT NULL and matches a previous exercise's group → add to existing superset
   - If `superset_group` is NULL or different → create new single-exercise group
   - Supersets are wrapped in `.superset-block` div with orange left border

**Template rendering** (workout.html:16-102):
```jinja2
{% for phase_name in ["warm-up", "skills", "main", "cool-down"] %}
    {% for group in phase_groups[phase_name] %}
        {% if group.is_superset %}
            <div class="superset-block">
        {% endif %}
        {% for ex in group.exercises %}
            <!-- Exercise card -->
        {% endfor %}
    {% endfor %}
{% endfor %}
```

---

### 6. UI COMPONENTS & STYLING

**Exercise Card Styling** (`style.css:224-361`):

```css
.exercise-card {
    background: #fff;
    border-radius: 8px;
    border: 1px solid #e0e0e0;
    padding: 1rem;
    margin-bottom: 0.75rem;
}

.instruction-card {  /* For mobility/stretch exercises */
    background: #fafafa;
    border-style: dashed;
}

.superset-block {
    border-left: 3px solid #fc4c02;  /* Orange accent */
    padding-left: 0.75rem;
}

.sets-table {
    display: flex;
    gap: 0.5rem;
}

.set-row input {
    padding: 0.5rem 0.25rem;
    border: 1px solid #e0e0e0;
    border-radius: 6px;
    min-height: 44px;  /* Mobile-friendly */
}
```

**Current Actions:**
- `Save Progress` button → saves all set data
- `Finish Workout` button → marks workout as completed
- `+ Add Exercise` button → modal for adding exercises mid-workout
- `+ Set` / `- Set` buttons per exercise → dynamic set rows
- `Remove exercise` (×) button

---

### 7. EXERCISE TYPES & SPECIAL HANDLING

**Exercise Types** (fitness_db.py:129):
```
strength, hold, skill, mobility, stretch
```

**Template Conditional** (workout.html:28):
```jinja2
{% if ex.exercise_type in ('mobility', 'stretch') %}
    <!-- Render as instruction card (no set logging) -->
{% else %}
    <!-- Render as regular exercise card (with sets table) -->
{% endif %}
```

**Instruction cards:**
- No set logging table
- Just show exercise name + duration (if applicable)
- Display notes/description

---

### 8. DATA FETCHING & SERVING

**Workout Detail Fetch** (app.py:300-356):
1. Get workout metadata (date, title, status, notes)
2. Query all workout_exercises with joins to exercises table
3. For each exercise, query logged sets from sets table
4. Group into phases and supersets on server side
5. Pass `phase_groups` dict to template

**Set Logging** (app.py:359-379):
- POST endpoint `/activity/w/<int:workout_id>/log`
- Accepts JSON: `{sets: [{workout_exercise_id, set_number, reps, duration_seconds, weight, rpe, notes}]}`
- Uses INSERT OR REPLACE to update existing sets
- Sets workout status to 'in_progress' if still 'planned'

---

### 9. FORMAT HELPER FUNCTION

**format_target()** (app.py:40-58):
Renders exercise target as human-readable string:
```python
def format_target(ex):
    """e.g., '3 x 8 reps @ 6.5kg RPE 7'"""
    parts = []
    if ex.get("target_duration_seconds"):
        parts.append(f"{ex['target_sets']} x {ex['target_duration_seconds']}s hold")
    elif ex.get("target_reps"):
        parts.append(f"{ex['target_sets']} x {ex['target_reps']}")
    if ex.get("target_weight"):
        parts.append(f"@ {ex['target_weight']}kg")
    if ex.get("target_rpe"):
        parts.append(f"RPE {ex['target_rpe']}")
    return " ".join(parts)
```

---

### 10. WORKOUT GENERATION (CONTEXT.MD)

**From `/Users/sumeet/matrix/web/argus/argus/domains/fitness/CONTEXT.md`:**

All strength exercises MUST include:
- Phase field (warm-up, skills, main, cool-down)
- target_sets, target_reps, or target_duration_seconds
- target_rpe (6-7 for most, never to failure)
- **rest_seconds** (included per exercise)
- superset_group numbers for paired exercises
- notes with coaching cues

Example from CONTEXT.md (line 69):
> "Include rest_seconds on each exercise"

---

### 11. STATIC FILES

**No JavaScript files found in `/static/`** — all JS is inline in templates (Jinja2 blocks)

**CSS file:** `/Users/sumeet/matrix/web/argus/argus/fitness_web/static/style.css`
- No external libraries (no Bootstrap, Tailwind, etc.)
- No Chart.js or other visualization libraries
- Pure semantic HTML + CSS

---

### 12. KEY INSIGHT: REST TIMER INTEGRATION POINT

**Where rest_seconds is available but unused:**

1. **In database:** rest_seconds field populated during workout creation ✓
2. **In Flask query:** Selected via `SELECT *` from workout_exercises ✓
3. **In template context:** Available as `ex.rest_seconds` (if exposed) ✓
4. **In UI:** NOT displayed, NO timer component exists ✗

**To build a rest timer, you would:**
1. Ensure `rest_seconds` is in the exercise dict sent to template (currently is, but not used)
2. Display it next to the target (e.g., "3 x 8 reps @ 6.5kg | Rest: 90s")
3. Add a timer UI component (can be simple vanilla JS timer)
4. Integrate timer trigger (on set completion, show rest countdown)

---

## SUMMARY

- **Current Tech**: Pure Flask + Jinja2 + Vanilla JS (no frameworks)
- **UI Type**: Server-rendered HTML with client-side form handling
- **Rest seconds storage**: Already in database, already used in workout generation, but never displayed or used in the UI
- **Exercise grouping**: By phase (5 sections) then by superset group within each phase
- **Set logging**: Real-time form inputs with 800ms debounced auto-save
- **No existing timers**: Instruction cards (mobility/stretch) are info-only; strength exercises only have set logging inputs
- **Styling**: Minimal, mobile-friendly CSS with focus on readability and accessibility (44px min-height inputs)
```

```
1	{% extends "base.html" %}
2	{% block title %}{{ workout.title }} — {{ workout.date }}{% endblock %}
3	
4	{% block content %}
5	<div class="workout-header">
6	    <h1>{{ workout.title }}</h1>
7	    <div class="meta">
8	        {{ workout.date }} <span class="badge" id="statusBadge">{{ workout.status }}</span>
9	        <form method="POST" action="/activity/w/{{ workout.id }}/delete" style="display:inline" onsubmit="return confirm('Delete this workout?')">
10	            <button type="submit" class="delete-btn">Delete</button>
11	        </form>
12	    </div>
13	    {% if workout.notes %}<p class="workout-notes">{{ workout.notes }}</p>{% endif %}
14	</div>
15	
16	{% set phase_labels = {"warm-up": "Warm-Up", "skills": "Skills", "main": "Main", "cool-down": "Cool-Down"} %}
17	{% for phase_name in ["warm-up", "skills", "main", "cool-down"] %}
18	{% if phase_groups.get(phase_name) %}
19	<h2 class="phase-heading">{{ phase_labels[phase_name] }}</h2>
20	
21	{% for group in phase_groups[phase_name] %}
22	{% if group.is_superset %}
23	<div class="superset-block">
24	    <div class="superset-label">Superset</div>
25	{% endif %}
26	
27	{% for ex in group.exercises %}
28	{% if ex.exercise_type in ('mobility', 'stretch') %}
29	<div class="exercise-card instruction-card" data-exercise-id="{{ ex.id }}">
30	    <div class="exercise-header">
31	        <div class="exercise-info">
32	            <span class="exercise-name">{{ ex.exercise_display_name or ex.exercise_name }}</span>
33	        </div>
34	        {% if ex.target_duration_seconds %}<span class="exercise-target">{{ ex.target_duration_seconds }}s</span>{% endif %}
35	        {% if ex.target_sets and ex.target_sets > 1 %}<span class="exercise-target">{{ ex.target_sets }} rounds</span>{% endif %}
36	        <button class="remove-exercise-btn" title="Remove">&times;</button>
37	    </div>
38	    <div class="exercise-note">{{ ex.notes or ex.exercise_description or '' }}</div>
39	</div>
40	{% else %}
41	<div class="exercise-card" data-exercise-id="{{ ex.id }}">
42	    <div class="exercise-header">
43	        <div class="exercise-info">
44	            <span class="exercise-name">{{ ex.exercise_display_name or ex.exercise_name }}</span>
45	            {% if ex.variation and ex.variation != 'standard' %}
46	            <span class="variation-badge">{{ ex.variation }}</span>
47	            {% endif %}
48	        </div>
49	        <span class="exercise-target">{{ format_target(ex) }}</span>
50	        <button class="remove-exercise-btn" title="Remove">&times;</button>
51	    </div>
52	    {% if ex.notes %}<div class="exercise-note">{{ ex.notes }}</div>{% endif %}
53	
54	    <div class="sets-table">
55	        <div class="sets-header">
56	            <span class="set-col">Set</span>
57	            {% if ex.target_duration_seconds %}
58	            <span class="input-col">Seconds</span>
59	            {% else %}
60	            <span class="input-col">Reps</span>
61	            {% endif %}
62	            {% if ex.target_weight %}<span class="input-col">kg</span>{% endif %}
63	            <span class="input-col-sm">RPE</span>
64	        </div>
65	        {% for s in range(1, (ex.target_sets or 3) + 1) %}
66	        <div class="set-row" data-set-number="{{ s }}">
67	            <span class="set-col set-number">{{ s }}</span>
68	            {% if ex.target_duration_seconds %}
69	            <input type="number" class="dur-input" inputmode="numeric" pattern="[0-9]*"
70	                   placeholder="{{ ex.target_duration_seconds }}"
71	                   value="{{ ex.sets[s].duration_seconds if s in ex.sets and ex.sets[s].duration_seconds else '' }}">
72	            {% else %}
73	            <input type="number" class="reps-input" inputmode="numeric" pattern="[0-9]*"
74	                   placeholder="{{ ex.target_reps or '' }}"
75	                   value="{{ ex.sets[s].reps if s in ex.sets and ex.sets[s].reps else '' }}">
76	            {% endif %}
77	            {% if ex.target_weight %}
78	            <input type="number" class="weight-input" inputmode="decimal" step="0.5"
79	                   placeholder="{{ ex.target_weight }}"
80	                   value="{{ ex.sets[s].weight if s in ex.sets and ex.sets[s].weight else '' }}">
81	            {% endif %}
82	            <input type="number" class="rpe-input" inputmode="decimal" step="0.5" min="1" max="10"
83	                   placeholder="{{ ex.target_rpe or '' }}"
84	                   value="{{ ex.sets[s].rpe if s in ex.sets and ex.sets[s].rpe else '' }}">
85	        </div>
86	        {% endfor %}
87	    </div>
88	    <div class="set-actions">
89	        <button class="set-action-btn add-set-btn" onclick="addSet(this)">+ Set</button>
90	        <button class="set-action-btn remove-set-btn" onclick="removeSet(this)">- Set</button>
91	    </div>
92	
93	    <textarea class="exercise-comment" placeholder="Notes..."></textarea>
94	</div>
95	{% endif %}
96	{% endfor %}
97	
98	{% if group.is_superset %}
99	</div>
100	{% endif %}
101	{% endfor %}
102	{% endif %}
103	{% endfor %}
104	
105	<button class="add-exercise-btn" id="addExerciseBtn">+ Add Exercise</button>
106	
107	<div class="modal" id="addExerciseModal" style="display:none">
108	    <div class="modal-content">
109	        <h3>Add Exercise</h3>
110	        <input type="text" id="newExerciseName" placeholder="Exercise name">
111	        <select id="newExerciseVariation">
112	            <option value="standard">Standard</option>
113	            <option value="negative">Negative</option>
114	            <option value="assisted">Assisted</option>
115	            <option value="band-assisted">Band-assisted</option>
116	            <option value="weighted">Weighted</option>
117	            <option value="half">Half rep</option>
118	            <option value="eccentric">Eccentric</option>
119	            <option value="wide">Wide</option>
120	            <option value="narrow">Narrow</option>
121	            <option value="diamond">Diamond</option>
122	            <option value="archer">Archer</option>
123	        </select>
124	        <div class="modal-row">
125	            <input type="number" id="newExerciseSets" placeholder="Sets" value="3" inputmode="numeric">
126	            <input type="number" id="newExerciseReps" placeholder="Reps" inputmode="numeric">
127	            <input type="number" id="newExerciseDuration" placeholder="Seconds" inputmode="numeric">
128	        </div>
129	        <div class="modal-actions">
130	            <button id="cancelAddExercise" class="btn-secondary">Cancel</button>
131	            <button id="confirmAddExercise" class="btn-primary">Add</button>
132	        </div>
133	    </div>
134	</div>
135	
136	<div class="workout-actions">
137	    <button class="save-btn" id="saveBtn">Save Progress</button>
138	    <button class="finish-btn" id="finishBtn">Finish Workout</button>
139	</div>
140	<div class="save-status" id="saveStatus"></div>
141	{% endblock %}
142	
143	{% block scripts %}
144	<script>
145	const WORKOUT_ID = {{ workout.id }};
146	let saveTimer = null;
147	
148	function collectSets() {
149	    const sets = [];
150	    document.querySelectorAll('.exercise-card').forEach(card => {
151	        const exerciseId = parseInt(card.dataset.exerciseId);
152	        card.querySelectorAll('.set-row').forEach(row => {
153	            const setNumber = parseInt(row.dataset.setNumber);
154	            const reps = row.querySelector('.reps-input')?.value || null;
155	            const dur = row.querySelector('.dur-input')?.value || null;
156	            const weight = row.querySelector('.weight-input')?.value || null;
157	            const rpe = row.querySelector('.rpe-input')?.value || null;
158	            if (reps || dur || weight || rpe) {
159	                sets.push({
160	                    workout_exercise_id: exerciseId,
161	                    set_number: setNumber,
162	                    reps: reps ? parseInt(reps) : null,
163	                    duration_seconds: dur ? parseInt(dur) : null,
164	                    weight: weight ? parseFloat(weight) : null,
165	                    rpe: rpe ? parseFloat(rpe) : null,
166	                });
167	            }
168	        });
169	    });
170	    return sets;
171	}
172	
173	function save() {
174	    const sets = collectSets();
175	    const status = document.getElementById('saveStatus');
176	    status.textContent = 'Saving...';
177	    fetch(`/activity/w/${WORKOUT_ID}/log`, {
178	        method: 'POST',
179	        headers: {'Content-Type': 'application/json'},
180	        body: JSON.stringify({sets}),
181	    })
182	    .then(r => r.json())
183	    .then(d => {
184	        status.textContent = d.ok ? 'Saved' : 'Error saving';
185	        if (d.ok) {
186	            const badge = document.getElementById('statusBadge');
187	            if (badge.textContent === 'planned') badge.textContent = 'in_progress';
188	        }
189	    })
190	    .catch(() => { status.textContent = 'Failed to save'; });
191	}
192	
193	function scheduleSave() {
194	    clearTimeout(saveTimer);
195	    document.getElementById('saveStatus').textContent = 'Unsaved changes...';
196	    saveTimer = setTimeout(save, 800);
197	}
198	
199	function addSet(btn) {
200	    const card = btn.closest('.exercise-card');
201	    const table = card.querySelector('.sets-table');
202	    const rows = table.querySelectorAll('.set-row');
203	    const lastRow = rows[rows.length - 1];
204	    const newNum = rows.length + 1;
205	    const clone = lastRow.cloneNode(true);
206	    clone.dataset.setNumber = newNum;
207	    clone.querySelector('.set-number').textContent = newNum;
208	    clone.querySelectorAll('input').forEach(inp => {
209	        inp.value = '';
210	        inp.addEventListener('input', scheduleSave);
211	    });
212	    table.appendChild(clone);
213	}
214	
215	function removeSet(btn) {
216	    const card = btn.closest('.exercise-card');
217	    const rows = card.querySelectorAll('.set-row');
218	    if (rows.length <= 1) return;
219	    rows[rows.length - 1].remove();
220	    scheduleSave();
221	}
222	
223	// Auto-save on input
224	document.querySelectorAll('.exercise-card input, .exercise-card textarea').forEach(el => {
225	    el.addEventListener('input', scheduleSave);
226	});
227	
228	// Manual save
229	document.getElementById('saveBtn').addEventListener('click', save);
230	
231	// Finish workout
232	document.getElementById('finishBtn').addEventListener('click', () => {
233	    save();
234	    setTimeout(() => {
235	        fetch(`/activity/w/${WORKOUT_ID}/status`, {
236	            method: 'POST',
237	            headers: {'Content-Type': 'application/json'},
238	            body: JSON.stringify({status: 'completed'}),
239	        })
240	        .then(r => r.json())
241	        .then(() => {
242	            document.getElementById('statusBadge').textContent = 'completed';
243	            document.getElementById('finishBtn').disabled = true;
244	            document.getElementById('finishBtn').textContent = 'Done';
245	            document.getElementById('saveStatus').textContent = 'Workout complete';
246	        });
247	    }, 1000);
248	});
249	
250	// Remove exercise
251	document.querySelectorAll('.remove-exercise-btn').forEach(btn => {
252	    btn.addEventListener('click', function() {
253	        const card = this.closest('.exercise-card');
254	        const eid = card.dataset.exerciseId;
255	        if (confirm('Remove this exercise?')) {
256	            fetch(`/activity/w/${WORKOUT_ID}/exercise/${eid}`, {method: 'DELETE'})
257	            .then(r => r.json())
258	            .then(() => card.remove());
259	        }
260	    });
261	});
262	
263	// Add exercise modal
264	document.getElementById('addExerciseBtn').addEventListener('click', () => {
265	    document.getElementById('addExerciseModal').style.display = 'flex';
266	});
267	document.getElementById('cancelAddExercise').addEventListener('click', () => {
268	    document.getElementById('addExerciseModal').style.display = 'none';
269	});
270	document.getElementById('confirmAddExercise').addEventListener('click', () => {
271	    const name = document.getElementById('newExerciseName').value.trim();
272	    if (!name) return;
273	    const data = {
274	        exercise_name: name,
275	        variation: document.getElementById('newExerciseVariation').value,
276	        target_sets: parseInt(document.getElementById('newExerciseSets').value) || 3,
277	        target_reps: parseInt(document.getElementById('newExerciseReps').value) || null,
278	        target_duration_seconds: parseInt(document.getElementById('newExerciseDuration').value) || null,
279	    };
280	    fetch(`/activity/w/${WORKOUT_ID}/exercise`, {
281	        method: 'POST',
282	        headers: {'Content-Type': 'application/json'},
283	        body: JSON.stringify(data),
284	    })
285	    .then(r => r.json())
286	    .then(ex => {
287	        document.getElementById('addExerciseModal').style.display = 'none';
288	        // Reload to show new exercise
289	        window.location.reload();
290	    });
291	});
292	</script>
293	{% endblock %}
294	
```

Pass `rest_seconds` to the template (`app.py`)\nThe query already uses `SELECT *` so `rest_seconds` is fetched — it just needs to be included in the exercise dict passed to the template. Add rest timer to each exercise card (`workout.html`)\nAdd a `data-rest-seconds=\"{{ ex.rest_seconds or 0 }}\"` attribute to each `.exercise-card` div (line 41). The JS timer reads this value.\n\n**File:** `argus/fitness_web/templates/workout.html`\n\n### 3. Add timer JavaScript (`workout.html` script block)\nAdd to the existing `<script>` block (starts at line 144):\n\n- `startRestTimer(setRow)` — called when a set input loses focus or gets a value\n  - Reads `rest_seconds` from the parent exercise card's data attribute\n  - If 0 or last set, skip\n  - Creates/shows a timer element below the set row\n  - Counts down every second, updates display\n  - On finish: play beep, show \"Go!\", auto-dismiss after 3s\n  - Tap to dismiss early\n\n- Timer triggers on input blur (not on every keystroke) — when the user finishes entering a value and moves on, the timer starts. This avoids timers firing while still typing.\n\n- Only one timer active at a time — starting a new one dismisses the previous.\n\n### 4. Add timer CSS (`style.css`)\nSimple countdown bar that sits between set rows:\n\n**File:** `argus/fitness_web/static/style.css`\n\n```css\n.rest-timer {\n    text-align: center;\n    padding: 0.75rem;\n    margin: 0.25rem 0;\n    background: #f0f7ff;\n    border-radius: 8px;\n    font-size: 1.5rem;\n    font-weight: 600;\n    color: #1a73e8;\n    cursor: pointer;\n    transition: background 0.3s;\n}\n.rest-timer.done {\n    background: #e8f5e9;\n    color: #2e7d32;\n    animation: pulse 0.5s ease-in-out 3;\n}\n@keyframes pulse {\n    50% { transform: scale(1.05); }\n}\n```\n\n### 5. Audio beep\nUse the Web Audio API to generate a short beep — no audio file needed. ~5 lines of JS to create an oscillator, play for 200ms.\n\n## Files to modify\n- `argus/fitness_web/app.py` — add rest seconds to format_target display (~1 line)\n- `argus/fitness_web/templates/workout.html` — add data attribute + timer JS (~40 lines)\n- `argus/fitness_web/static/style.css` — timer styles (~15 lines)\n\n## UX flow\n1. User opens workout at fit.argbox.net\n2. Each exercise shows target including rest time: \"3 x 8 reps · 90s rest\"\n3. User fills in set 1 (reps, weight, RPE) and taps out of the input\n4. Timer appears below set 1: \"90\" → \"89\" → \"88\" → ...\n5. At 0: beep sound, timer shows \"Go!\" in green\n6. User taps timer to dismiss (or it auto-dismisses after 3s)\n7. User fills in set 2, timer starts again\n8. No timer after the last set\n\n## Verification\n- Create a test workout with rest_seconds on exercises\n- Open on phone browser (primary use case)\n- Verify timer starts after entering a set value\n- Verify beep plays on completion\n- Verify tap-to-dismiss works\n- Verify no timer on last set\n- Verify exercises without rest_seconds show no timer\n"
}

## Approved Plan:
# Add Rest Timer to Workout Page

## Context
The workout page at fit.argbox.net shows exercises with set logging, but there's no rest timer between sets. The `rest_seconds` field already exists in the database and is populated by the AI workout generator — it's just never displayed or used in the UI. Adding a timer will help Sumeet stay on track during workouts and know exactly how long to rest.

## How it works

When a user fills in a set (enters reps, weight, or RPE), a rest timer automatically appears below that set row, counting down from the exercise's `rest_seconds` value. The timer shows a visual countdown, and when it finishes, a short beep sound plays and the timer text flashes to say "Go!". The user can tap the timer to dismiss it early.

No timer appears for the last set (nothing to rest before), or if the exercise has no `rest_seconds` value.

## Changes

### 1. Pass `rest_seconds` to the template (`app.py`)
The query already uses `SELECT *` so `rest_seconds` is fetched — it just needs to be included in the exercise dict passed to the template. Check that `format_target()` can optionally show rest time.

**File:** `argus/fitness_web/app.py`
- In `format_target()` (~line 40): append rest info to target string, e.g. "3 x 8 reps @ 6.5kg RPE 7 · 90s rest"
- No other backend changes needed — the data is already there.

### 2. Add rest timer to each exercise card (`workout.html`)
Add a `data-rest-seconds="{{ ex.rest_seconds or 0 }}"` attribute to each `.exercise-card` div (line 41). The JS timer reads this value.

**File:** `argus/fitness_web/templates/workout.html`

### 3. Add timer JavaScript (`workout.html` script block)
Add to the existing `<script>` block (starts at line 144):

- `startRestTimer(setRow)` — called when a set input loses focus or gets a value
  - Reads `rest_seconds` from the parent exercise card's data attribute
  - If 0 or last set, skip
  - Creates/shows a timer element below the set row
  - Counts down every second, updates display
  - On finish: play beep, show "Go!", auto-dismiss after 3s
  - Tap to dismiss early

- Timer triggers on input blur (not on every keystroke) — when the user finishes entering a value and moves on, the timer starts. This avoids timers firing while still typing.

- Only one timer active at a time — starting a new one dismisses the previous.

### 4. Add timer CSS (`style.css`)
Simple countdown bar that sits between set rows:

**File:** `argus/fitness_web/static/style.css`

```css
.rest-timer {
    text-align: center;
    padding: 0.75rem;
    margin: 0.25rem 0;
    background: #f0f7ff;
    border-radius: 8px;
    font-size: 1.5rem;
    font-weight: 600;
    color: #1a73e8;
    cursor: pointer;
    transition: background 0.3s;
}
.rest-timer.done {
    background: #e8f5e9;
    color: #2e7d32;
    animation: pulse 0.5s ease-in-out 3;
}
@keyframes pulse {
    50% { transform: scale(1.05); }
}
```

### 5. Audio beep
Use the Web Audio API to generate a short beep — no audio file needed. ~5 lines of JS to create an oscillator, play for 200ms.

## Files to modify
- `argus/fitness_web/app.py` — add rest seconds to format_target display (~1 line)
- `argus/fitness_web/templates/workout.html` — add data attribute + timer JS (~40 lines)
- `argus/fitness_web/static/style.css` — timer styles (~15 lines)

## UX flow
1. User opens workout at fit.argbox.net
2. Each exercise shows target including rest time: "3 x 8 reps · 90s rest"
3. User fills in set 1 (reps, weight, RPE) and taps out of the input
4. Timer appears below set 1: "90" → "89" → "88" → ...
5. At 0: beep sound, timer shows "Go!" in green
6. User taps timer to dismiss (or it auto-dismisses after 3s)
7. User fills in set 2, timer starts again
8. No timer after the last set

## Verification
- Create a test workout with rest_seconds on exercises
- Open on phone browser (primary use case)
- Verify timer starts after entering a set value
- Verify beep plays on completion
- Verify tap-to-dismiss works
- Verify no timer on last set
- Verify exercises without rest_seconds show no timer

{

  "old_string": "// Add exercise modal\ndocument.getElementById('addExerciseBtn').addEventListener('click', () => {",
  "new_string": "// Rest timer\nlet activeTimer = null;\nlet activeTimerInterval = null;\n\nfunction playBeep() {\n    try {\n        const ctx = new (window.AudioContext || window.webkitAudioContext)();\n        const osc = ctx.createOscillator();\n        const gain = ctx.createGain();\n        osc.connect(gain);\n        gain.connect(ctx.destination);\n        osc.frequency.value = 880;\n        gain.gain.value = 0.3;\n        osc.start();\n        osc.stop(ctx.currentTime + 0.2);\n    } catch(e) {}\n}\n\nfunction dismissTimer() {\n    if (activeTimer) {\n        activeTimer.remove();\n        activeTimer = null;\n    }\n    if (activeTimerInterval) {\n        clearInterval(activeTimerInterval);\n        activeTimerInterval = null;\n    }\n}\n\nfunction startRestTimer(setRow) {\n    dismissTimer();\n    const card = setRow.closest('.exercise-card');\n    const restSeconds = parseInt(card.dataset.restSeconds) || 0;\n    if (restSeconds <= 0) return;\n\n    // Don't show timer for the last set\n    const allRows = card.querySelectorAll('.set-row');\n    const lastRow = allRows[allRows.length - 1];\n    if (setRow === lastRow) return;\n\n    // Check the set actually has data entered\n    const inputs = setRow.querySelectorAll('input');\n    const hasData = Array.from(inputs).some(i => i.value);\n    if (!hasData) return;\n\n    const timer = document.createElement('div');\n    timer.className = 'rest-timer';\n    let remaining = restSeconds;\n    timer.textContent = remaining;\n    timer.addEventListener('click', dismissTimer);\n    setRow.after(timer);\n    activeTimer = timer;\n\n    activeTimerInterval = setInterval(() => {\n        remaining--;\n        if (remaining <= 0) {\n            clearInterval(activeTimerInterval);\n            activeTimerInterval = null;\n            timer.textContent = 'Go!';\n            timer.classList.add('done');\n            playBeep();\n            setTimeout(dismissTimer, 3000);\n        } else {\n            timer.textContent = remaining;\n        }\n    }, 1000);\n}\n\n// Trigger timer on input blur (when user finishes entering a value)\ndocument.querySelectorAll('.exercise-card .set-row input').forEach(input => {\n    input.addEventListener('blur', function() {\n        if (this.value) {\n            startRestTimer(this.closest('.set-row'));\n        }\n    });\n});\n\n// Add exercise modal\ndocument.getElementById('addExerciseBtn').addEventListener('click', () => {"
}

| Date | Activity |

**What's going well:**
- Pace is consistent (6:43 → 6:49 — basically the same, good)
- Alternating run / strength / run pattern
- Two cardio + one strength in 6 days is a sensible restart load

**Today's run → tomorrow's strength** spacing is solid. 2 days since your last run, 3 days since strength — you're well rested.

**One thing worth tracking:** RHR of 89 is on the higher side (normal range 60-80). Could be the restart phase, stress, sleep, or hydration — log it again after today's run and see if it trends down over the next couple of weeks.

For today's run — given your last two were both around 4km @ 6:45/km, you could either:
- Repeat the pattern (consistency, build the base)
- Push a bit on distance (5km same pace) — your HR data is missing from Strava but the pace suggests you have room

Have a good one.

> DEVELOPER

But am I doing good considering i was starting from not training for ages?

> AGENT

Yeah honestly mate, you're doing great for a restart.

Think about what you've actually done in 2 weeks:
- **3 sessions** (2 runs + 1 full-body strength)
- **21 sets logged** in the strength session — that's a proper workout, not just going through the motions
- **Pace is already at 6:45/km** after months off — that's a respectable running pace, not couch-to-5k territory
- **Same pace twice in a row** — your body is finding its rhythm, not dropping off

The biggest win is **showing up consistently without overdoing it**. The classic restart mistake is going hard for a week, getting sore or injured, and quitting. You're doing the opposite — easy, steady, building a base. That's exactly how to do it.

The fact that you're planning the next two sessions (run today, strength tomorrow) means you're treating it like a routine, not a one-off motivation burst. That's the part that actually matters long-term.

Keep it boring for the next 4-6 weeks. Don't try to PR anything. Just keep showing up.

> AGENT

**Lunch Run — 2026-05-14**
- **4.21km in 29:32** @ avg 7:00/km
- No HR data this time (forgot the watch/strap?)

**Splits:**

| KM | Pace | Elev |
|----|------|------|
| 1 | 7:03/km | +2.1m |
| 2 | 6:21/km | +2.8m |
| 3 | 6:36/km | -4.3m |
| 4 | **8:02/km** | flat |
| 5 (0.2km) | 6:44/km | -0.8m |

**Feedback:**

- **Slightly slower overall** (7:00/km vs 6:43-6:49 the previous two). Not a worry — could be heat, sleep, or just legs.
- **KM 1 was a warm-up pace** (good — you started easy)
- **KM 2 was your fastest** at 6:21/km, even on an uphill. That's your real pace.
- **KM 4 was the killer** — dropped to 8:02/km on flat ground. That's a 100s/km slowdown. Either you stopped for traffic, hit a wall, or your legs went. The strong finish in the last bit (6:44) suggests it wasn't pure exhaustion — more likely a break.
- **Negative split-ish finish** — you had energy left at the end.

**Bottom line:** Solid run for a 3rd session in a week. The km 4 slowdown stands out — if it was a traffic stop, ignore it. If it was your legs, that's normal fatigue from this being your 3rd cardio in 6 days.

**Tomorrow's strength workout** — I'd say go for it. You're not too cooked, and the strong finish today suggests you've got more in the tank than the average pace shows. Just don't go heavy on legs.

Wear the HR strap next time — without it we're guessing at effort.
